## Abstract

Take-Two carries 2,519.8 of gross borrowings against 1,149.4 of cash — net debt of 1,370.4 on the strict basis, or 2.02x a company-defined, non-GAAP trailing EBITDA of 677.6 and 2.42x through the cycle, after leverage fell from 5.81x and then rose again. The wall is near: 56.5% of principal falls due within 24 months, covered 1.17x by cash and bank deposits with no market access. Liquidity runs 31.0 months on the conservative basis, and the one covenant — a 3.75x leverage ceiling — sits at 1.07x, or +53.8% headroom on conservative netting. Stress cash and earnings together and that covenant breaks near a 46% EBITDA fall. Verdict: adequate, with the 24-month refinancing resting on one unreported product cycle.

---

## 1. Solvency Verdict

- **Verdict: Adequate** — leverage elevated but serviceable; coverage and covenant headroom acceptable on the contract's own definitions; refinancing and covenant attention needed because 56.5% of principal matures inside 24 months and the cash that repays it is replenished by one product cycle. *(Not "Solid": maturities are **not** laddered — weighted-average maturity is 2.93 years — the refinancing is not secured by any announced issuance, GAAP operating profit is a **loss** of (180.8) over the trailing twelve months, fixed-charge coverage is **0.44x**, and cash conversion is 50.6%. Not "Stretched": covenant headroom is wide on both netting cases (+71.4% / +53.8%), the 24-month wall is covered 1.17x by cash and deposits before any lender, 100% of drawn debt is fixed-rate and unsecured with no borrowing base, and a −30%/−40% EBITDA shock is survived with no waiver, equity raise or asset sale.)*
- **Net leverage (net debt / EBITDA): 2.02x** — strict §15 basis (gross borrowings at carrying value 2,519.8 − cash & equivalents 1,149.4 = **1,370.4**) over **company-defined, non-GAAP** LTM EBITDA to 2026-09-30 of **677.6** (an engine-computed bridge on the company's own published definition). On the **vendor** LTM EBITDA of 724.9: **1.89x**. On **mid-cycle / normalised** EBITDA of 565.3 (own five-year average FY2022–FY2026): **2.42x — the central estimate**. On **company-guided FY2027** EBITDA of 1,024–1,082 (midpoint 1,053, a **forecast**, the coming Grand Theft Auto VI peak): **1.30x — a floor, not a forecast**. On the **cash-backed** base of 373.2 (677.6 less the 304.4 gap between 716.3 of cash content spend and 411.9 of amortisation): **3.67x**. **Gross leverage, stated alongside: 3.72x** gross debt / EBITDA on 677.6 (3.48x on 724.9; 4.46x mid-cycle; **4.30x** on the lease-inclusive gross-debt variant of 2,911.4). **There is no GAAP EBITDA** — the GAAP operating result is a loss on both the latest full year ((104.2)) and the LTM ((180.8)) — so every multiple in this module rests on a **non-GAAP** EBITDA, and the **broad** net-debt figure (846.2, which also nets 524.2 of liquid short-term investments) and the **vendor** figure (1,237.8, broad **and** lease-inclusive) are never interchangeable with the canonical strict figure. [`01` §4, §5b, §7]
- **Liquidity runway: 31.0 months** on the conservative **gross-obligations** basis (committed liquidity 2,671.5 ÷ monthly burn 86.2, where annual burn 1,034.2 = 12-month maturities 629.4 + cash interest 114.8 + the whole 290.0 guided capex standing in for undisclosed maintenance capex + 0.0 committed dividends/buybacks, with **no FCF netted**). **19.4 months on in-hand liquidity alone** (1,673.6 of cash plus liquid bank deposits, no revolver). Net-of-FCF cases span **56.6 months** (realised LTM FCF +62.6), **37.1 months** (worst audited year, FY2025 FCF (234.9) — a realised base rate), **23.9 months** (zero-mitigation bound annualising the latest half-year's (356.2)), and **no finite runway** if the company's own FY2027 guidance lands. Honest range: **24 to 57 months committed / 15 to 35 months in-hand**. Peak intra-quarter working-capital need is **not disclosed**, so every figure is the **upper** end of its case. [`03` §3c, §3d]
- **Cash-finance-cost bridge:** **source classification is RESOLVED, on a reported basis.** The chosen FCF is **reported §15 FCF = CFO − total capex** (no explicitly adjusted FCF measure exists in this pool, and **no proxy is used**, so there is no proxy-netted interest to double-charge). Classification was **established, not assumed**, by a direct read of all three sections of the FY26 10-K cash-flow statement: cash interest (**153.0** paid in FY2026; **57.4** of Senior Notes interest payments in H1 FY27) and operating-lease cash payments (**82.4** in FY2026) are **all inside operating cash flow** — the investing section carries no interest or lease line and the financing section carries no interest payment. **Cash finance costs absent from the chosen FCF = 0.0**, so on the net-of-FCF basis nothing is re-added for interest, leases or capex; on the gross-obligations basis the **114.8** forward coupon is used *instead of*, never in addition to, that zero. Each payment is counted once. **Unresolved limits retained:** the Q2 FY27 10-Q carries no supplemental "interest paid" line (closed by arithmetic — 57.4 is exactly half the 114.8 annual coupon schedule); **revolver commitment and letter-of-credit fees are not disclosed anywhere in the pool and are not assumed to be zero**; capitalised interest is not disclosed either way; no finance leases exist, so there is no lease principal/interest split to untangle. [`02` §4; `03` §3b; `04` §1]
- **Maturity wall (% within 24 months): 56.5%** — 1,429.4 of the 2,529.4 of filed principal, measured to 2028-09-30 and unchanged measured from today. 24.9% (629.4) falls due within 12 months. Weighted-average maturity **2.93 years** from 2026-09-30 (2.79 from today). Largest single fiscal year: **FY2028, 1,400.0 = 55.3%** — but that is **two payments eleven and a half months apart**, 600.0 on 2027-04-14 and 800.0 on 2028-03-28, not one cliff; both readings travel together. Cover: 12-month maturities covered **1.83x** by cash & equivalents alone and **2.66x** by cash plus liquid investments; 24-month wall covered **1.17x** by cash plus liquid investments with no market access and **1.87x** including the 997.9 of committed, non-borrowing-base revolver availability. Refinancing step-up roughly **+90 to +145 bps** over the 4.59% weighted-average coupon (the +136/+145 bp leg is a **bound** built from web-sourced, indicative, unverified, mismatched-tenor inputs). [`02` §1–§5]
- **Tightest covenant + headroom:** **Maximum "Leverage Ratio" of 3.75 : 1.00**, §6.05 of the 2022 Credit Agreement as amended 2025-05-19, tested at every fiscal-quarter end, **permanently active and not springing** — and tightest **by default, because it is the only maintenance financial covenant in the entire capital structure** (no interest-coverage, no minimum-liquidity, no net-worth test; the Senior Notes indenture carries none). Actual **1.07x → +71.4% headroom** on full contractual cash netting; **1.73x → +53.8%** on the conservative cash-only-netting case (used because whether the 524.2 of bank time deposits qualifies as "Permitted Investments" is **not proven from available data**); **+63.3%** on a mid-cycle covenant-EBITDA proxy; **+15.4%** with no netting at all, which sizes how much of the cushion the netting clause creates. **Every covenant figure is the engine's own calculation on the contract's own definitions, not a company-reported figure** — neither filing discloses the computed ratio and the §5.01(c) compliance certificate is not in the pool. Covenant EBITDA of **797.2** is a deliberate **floor** (seven uncapped add-back clauses omitted). **No equity cure right exists.** A dated, mechanical tightening lands on **2026-12-31** (Maximum Cash Netting Amount steps down 2,000.0 → 1,500.0), costing about **4.8 percentage points** of headroom from the quarter ending 2027-03-31, with no change in trading and no mention in either filing's narrative. [`04` §2–§3]
- **Stress break point (EBITDA decline that breaks it): about 46% — covenant first, not the cash box.** On the conservative anchor (joint cash-and-EBITDA stress, cash-only netting, proportional covenant-EBITDA haircut) the 3.75x ceiling breaches at a **45.6%** covenant-EBITDA decline; **59.9%** on full netting; **68.2%** / **51.9%** on the absolute pass-through variants; **71.4%** if cash is frozen at today's balance (the static figure, which understates fragility because this covenant's numerator **rises** as cash drains). First *visible* strain is earlier: a **27.3%** decline empties cash plus bank deposits over 24 months and forces a revolver draw. **Committed liquidity is not exhausted by an EBITDA decline alone** in either window (h = 310.6% at 12 months, 100.9% at 24 — reported plainly as h ≥ 1, not as a fabricated percentage). **−30% and −40% are survived with no waiver, no equity raise and no asset sale**; **−60% is not** (covenant breaches at +12 months on both netting cases, and the 443.7 revolver draw the 24-month gap needs exceeds the 95.4 the covenant would permit). **−40% plus a repeat of the LTM 225.0 deferred-revenue outflow breaches the conservative anchor outright (−1.1%).** Every scenario is a **zero-mitigation bound**; the measured realised-offset case (+178.5 of annual cost response = 72.0 guided opex cut + 106.5 from a realised −160 bp product-cost ratio on FY2026 revenue of 6,656.4, both qualifiers attached) turns a −40% shock into a **−13.7%** effective one and is the expected-outcome read. [`06` §2–§4]
- **Solvency strength /100: 61** *(higher = better; no cap applied — see §4)*
- **Liquidity runway /100: 68** *(higher = better)*
- **Refinancing risk /100: 45** — **INVERTED: higher = WORSE**
- **Covenant headroom /100: 68** *(higher = better; assessable — covenant disclosure is present and numeric)*
- **Downside resilience /100: 58** *(higher = better; assessable — the stress test ran in full)*
- **Data quality /100: 80** *(higher = better; triage verdict **Sufficient**, no partial-data cap binds)*
- **Overall usefulness /100: 84** *(higher = better)*
- **Biggest solvency risk (one line):** the covenant's cushion is contractual **cash netting**, so money leaving the balance sheet tightens it faster than earnings falling — and the entire 24-month funding plan reduces to one unreported product cycle, with realised LTM free cash flow of **62.6** and a just-reported half-year of **(356.2)** behind it.

## 1A. Module Disconfirmation *(CLAUDE.md §8; fix F37)*

- **Strongest bear point:** the cash engine behind the structure is roughly half what the EBITDA headline implies, and the next two years' funding depends on it reversing in one product cycle. CFO/EBITDA was **50.6%** over the LTM and below 70% in five of six periods [`earnings/06` via `04` §1]; free cash flow was negative in **three of the last five fiscal years** ((185.2) FY2024, (234.9) FY2025, (356.2) H1 FY27); and on the cash-backed EBITDA base of **373.2** net leverage is already **3.67x today** and crosses **6.12x at a −40% haircut** [`06` §2]. For guided FY2027 operating cash flow of **over 1,000.0** to land after a first half that printed **(173.5)**, the six months to 2027-03-31 must generate **over 1,173.5** — against a best-ever full year of **434.1** [`03` §3a].
- **Strongest bull point (the steelman):** the debt terms are about as benign as a levered structure gets, and the near wall is already paid for. **100% of drawn borrowings are fixed-rate** (weighted-average coupon 4.59%, no swaps needed), **100% unsecured with no collateral and no borrowing base**, **exactly one maintenance covenant** with **no springing trigger, no minimum-liquidity test and no interest-coverage test**, a **committed 1,000.0 revolver to 2030-05-19 with 997.9 available** and two one-year extension options, **no dividend and no committed buyback**, and the 12-month maturities of 629.4 covered **1.83x by cash & equivalents alone** [`01` §1, §7; `02` §3–§4; `04` §2; `03` §1]. A **+200 bp** rate shock changes drawn interest cost by **$0.0**, and the company's net interest exposure runs the *other* way — it earned **90.0** of interest income over the LTM, so falling rates are the adverse direction [`02` §3].
- **Single killer risk (solvency-specific):** the **cash-only-netting covenant anchor at −40% plus a working-capital shock**. The 3.75x ceiling is the only financial test that can be failed, its cushion is mostly the contractual netting of cash, there is **no equity cure right** — a breach needs a lender waiver or amendment — and the conservative anchor already sits at **+4.7%** of headroom in the own-history −43.4% scenario and at **−1.1%** at −40% plus a repeat of the LTM 225.0 deferred-revenue outflow [`04` §2, §3; `06` §2].
- **Disconfirming evidence already visible:** plenty, in both directions. (a) Management's own framing does **not** reconcile with the engine's numbers and is **not relied on** — on the Q2 FY27 call the Executive Chairman said the balance sheet is "aimed at being very conservative and very strong… we're geared at less than 1x LTM EBITDA", which is below **every** basis computed (strict 2.02x / 1.89x, broad 1.25x / 1.17x, vendor broad-and-lease-inclusive 1.71x), with no stated basis [`02` §5]. (b) The vendor credit panel puts TTWO in the **bottom quartile on all four of overall, operational, solvency and liquidity** against 25 interactive-entertainment peers, alongside an **S&P BBB** indication — both tier-5 vendor reads, with **no rating-agency rationale or outlook in the pool**, so no rating trajectory may be inferred [`01` §7; `02` §5]. (c) Against the bear case, the engine's own covenant build is deliberately conservative — covenant EBITDA of 797.2 omits seven uncapped add-back clauses and the broad clause-(c) reading that would lift it to 1,209.1, so the error in 1.07x runs toward **too little** headroom, not too much [`04` §6 qualifier 3]. (d) The measured realised-offset case (+178.5) shows a −40% shock becoming −13.7% effective with the 24-month liquidity gap disappearing — the mitigated base rate beside the zero-mitigation bound [`06` §2].

Headroom quality LOW-to-MEDIUM** | **Accrual coverage and cash coverage tell opposite stories about the same twelve months, and both are true:** EBITDA/interest **4.99x** (gross interest 135.7) — but EBIT/interest is **(1.33)x** because EBIT is negative, (EBITDA − total capex)/interest is **2.75x**, the content-adjusted figure is **0.51x**, and **fixed-charge coverage is 0.44x**. **The covenant is more sensitive to cash leaving than to EBITDA falling** — removing the netting alone takes headroom from +71.4% to +15.4%, so the breach point moves to a **57% / 43% / 29%** EBITDA fall as cash runs to 75% / 50% / 25% of 1,673.6. **No equity cure right.** It also **corrected an upstream misread by name**: the netting allowance is the **Maximum Cash Netting Amount (2,000.0 until 2026-12-31, then 1,500.0)**, not the struck 1,000.0 figure `00` recorded — and that dated step-down costs about **4.8 percentage points** of headroom, visible only in the exhibit |

The genuinely contingent exposure is the **tax file: 274.5 of gross unrecognized tax benefits including 55.6 of accrued interest and penalties, with eight fiscal years (FY2018–FY2025) currently under examination** and the company saying amounts paid "may differ materially from the amounts accrued". **Three classic hidden exposures are genuinely clean**: no defined-benefit deficit, **nil receivables sold** under the uncommitted 215.0 facility in FY2026 or H1 FY27, and no structured-finance / variable-interest entities asserted. **Five items have no quantified ceiling** (litigation above the accrual — "reasonably possible"; tax above 274.5; the **redacted** Microsoft platform defence-of-claims cap; repatriation tax on 945.0 offshore; surety bonds not disclosed either way). ## 3. Reconciliation

Eight items where specialists, sources or management differed. In each case the more conservative (more fragile) reading is carried, and the disagreement is stated rather than averaged away.

| # | Disagreement | Side A | Side B | Reconciled (more conservative) view |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Net-debt basis** | `01`: strict §15 **1,370.4** (gross carrying borrowings − cash & equivalents only) | `ciq_facts.json` / CIQ Balance Sheet: **1,237.8**, and the vendor's "Total Debt" 2,911.4 | **Not a conflict — a definitional difference that reconciles exactly with no residual:** 1,370.4 − 524.2 short-term investments + 391.6 operating leases = 1,237.8. The vendor figure is **broad on the cash side AND lease-inclusive on the debt side** (two departures from §15 in one number). `01` designates **strict 1,370.4 as canonical** and every downstream agent used it. The broad figure (846.2) and the vendor figure are carried only with both labels attached and are **never interchangeable** |
| 2 | **Which EBITDA leads** | `01`(A): **677.6**, company's own published non-GAAP definition, built from the FY26 10-K's own lines (FY2026 component 760.6 is **engine-computed**) | `01`(B): vendor **724.9** — my read of that tab matches exactly, so there is no misread to flag | **(A) 677.6 leads** (filing-derived, on the company's own definition, §4/§5) and sits **6.5% below** the vendor. The vendor figure is **cited for its own number and not overridden**; both appear on every ratio. Using (A) produces the **higher** (more fragile) leverage — the conservative choice |
| 3 | **Vendor leverage ratios look lower** | CIQ Ratios tab: Total Debt/EBITDA **3.639x**, Net Debt/EBITDA **1.547x** (LTM) | `01`: 3.72x / 2.02x on (A) | **Named and rejected with the reason:** the vendor's implied denominator is ~799.9 at LTM and **exactly 868.1 at FY2026 — that tab's own EBITDA**R** line (EBITDA before rent)**. The vendor Ratios tab is computed on **EBITDAR**. `01` uses **EBITDA, not EBITDAR**, throughout |
| 4 | **Covenant cash-netting allowance** | `00` §5 note 3: netting "up to **$1,000,000,000**" | `04` §2: the operative term is the **Maximum Cash Netting Amount — 2,000.0 until and including 2026-12-31, then 1,500.0** | **`04` wins, on evidence, and corrects `00` by name.** The frozen extract of Exhibit 10.41 is a **conformed redline** with struck and inserted text run together; the FY26 10-K's own narrative confirms the *inserted* side of every other redline pair (facility 1,000.0, maturity 2030-05-19), so the inserted side is operative and the 1,000.0 is the **deleted** figure. The correction cuts **both** ways: it widens today's headroom **and** introduces a dated tightening on 2026-12-31 worth about **4.8 percentage points**. The underlying redline ambiguity is retained as a real limitation on the covenant text |
| 5 | **Covenant headroom: which netting case** | `04` base: full netting → **1.07x, +71.4%** | `04` conservative: cash-only netting → **1.73x, +53.8%** | **Both carried; the cash-only case is the anchor.** Whether the 524.2 of bank time deposits meets the contract's "Permitted Investments" two-year and $250,000,000-capital tests is **not proven from available data** — the filing names neither the banks nor the deposit maturities. `04` instructed `06` to use cash-only netting as its conservative anchor, and `06` did |
| 6 | **Stress break point: which figure** | `04` §3 static: breach at a **71.4%** EBITDA fall (cash frozen at today's balance) | `06` §3 joint: **59.9%** full netting, **45.6%** cash-only — numerator **rises** as cash drains | **The joint solve supersedes the static one, and 45.6% is the headline.** `04` qualifier 8 required cash and EBITDA to be stressed together precisely because contractual netting supplies most of the cushion; quoting 71.4% alone would overstate resilience by roughly 26 percentage points |
| 7 | **"Self-funded" vs "one product cycle"** | `02` §4 conclusion: "self-funded / low refi risk" | `03` §5 and `06` §4: the whole 24-month plan reduces to one unreported launch | **Both are true on different horizons, and `02` itself attached the qualifier:** the next **12 months are pre-funded from the balance sheet** (629.4 due against 1,149.4 of cash, 1.83x) and **nothing breaks under a 12-month market closure at any haircut tested**; the **24-month** wall is payable from liquidity but leaves about **244.2** and must then be replenished by Grand Theft Auto VI or by the market. The label "self-funded" may **not** travel without "a statement about the cash balance at 2026-09-30, not about cash generation" |
| 8 | **Management's leverage framing** | Q2 FY27 call, Executive Chairman: balance sheet "aimed at being very conservative and very strong… By year-end, we expect to be in a net cash position… right now, we're geared at less than 1x LTM EBITDA" | `01`: strict **2.02x / 1.89x**; broad 1.25x / 1.17x; vendor broad-and-lease-inclusive **1.71x** | **Recorded as an unreconciled management figure, not as evidence.** "Less than 1x" is below **every** basis computed and **no basis is stated** for it. The forward "net cash position" claim is a management forecast; `01` and `06` both record TTWO as **not net cash on either basis**, so the §24 Filter-3 net-cash credit is **not** granted |

One further item is a **correction of an upstream figure rather than a disagreement**, and it is kept visible: `00` recorded the FY26 lease liability as 440.3 (the 2026-03-31 figure); `01` refreshed it to **391.6 at 2026-09-30** and used the refreshed figure throughout, while `05` deliberately held its contingency ratios on a **matched 2026-03-31 basis** (exposures and equity both at that date) because no refreshed undiscounted lease schedule or tax-contingency figure exists at 2026-09-30. Both are right on their own stated basis; neither was mixed into a single ratio.

## 3A. Fragility Map (what breaks first)

| Fragility Driver | Indicator | Current Status | Why It Matters |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Maturity concentration** | % due within 24m | **56.5%** (1,429.4 of 2,529.4 principal); 24.9% within 12m; weighted-average maturity **2.93 years**; largest fiscal year 1,400.0 = 55.3%, but as **two payments 11.5 months apart** (2027-04-14 and 2028-03-28) | Wall risk is real but **pre-funded on the balance sheet, not on cash generation**: 24-month cover is 1.17x on cash plus deposits and 1.87x with the revolver. Paying it from liquidity leaves ~244.2 against realised LTM FCF of 62.6 |
| **Availability liquidity** | usable liquidity vs uses | **2,671.5 committed** (cash 1,149.4 + liquid deposits 524.2 + **stated** revolver availability 997.9) against 12-month gross obligations of **1,034.2 → 2.58x**, i.e. **31.0 months**. Restricted cash 83.0 and **494.0 of uncommitted capacity excluded**. Revolver is **not borrowing-base**, availability is **disclosed in the filing**, no minimum-liquidity covenant to subtract | Revolver reality is unusually clean here — availability is a filed number, not an estimate — but **37.4% of the cushion is covenant-conditional** and **82.2% of the cash (945.0) is offshore with an unquantified repatriation tax**. Peak intra-quarter need **not disclosed**, so figures are upper bounds |
| **Covenant illusion risk** | covenant EBITDA vs reported | Covenant Consolidated Adjusted EBITDA **797.2** (engine build, a **floor**) vs company-defined **677.6** (+17.6%) and vendor **724.9** (+10.0%); vs the only GAAP anchor, an LTM operating **loss of (180.8)**. Largest single driver: **344.7 of uncapped stock-based compensation = 50.9% of the (A) EBITDA base**. Clause (s) imports unknown add-backs from JPMorgan "Models" **not in this pool**. Broad clause-(c) reading would lift covenant EBITDA to **1,209.1** | **Add-back risk points the WRONG way for a bear and the right way for conservatism:** the omitted clauses would *widen* headroom, so error in 1.07x runs toward **too little** headroom. The real illusion risk is a reader mistaking **71.4% of covenant headroom for 71.4% of cash headroom** — the same company shows fixed-charge coverage of **0.44x** and content-adjusted interest coverage of **0.51x** |
| **Floating-rate sensitivity** | floating % net of hedges | **0.0% of drawn borrowings float** (all 2,529.4 of principal fixed, coupons 3.70/4.95/5.40/4.00/5.60/0.00%, weighted average **4.59%**); **no interest-rate swaps disclosed**; the only floating instrument is the **undrawn** revolver | A **+200 bp** shock costs **$0.0** on drawn debt. Exposure runs the **opposite** way: 90.0 of LTM interest income on the cash pile means **falling** rates are adverse. Contingent only: +200 bp on a full 997.9 draw would cost 20.0 a year. Repricing happens only at refinancing, at roughly **+90 to +145 bps** |
| **Structural subordination** | HoldCo debt vs upstreaming | **29.4 of face borrowings (1.2%) sits at Zynga Inc. (OpCo)** and is **structurally senior** as to Zynga's assets, Take-Two-guaranteed — and it **matures 2026-12-15**, within weeks. 2,500.0 of Senior Notes (98.8%) and the revolver sit at the **parent that owns the group**. **No dividend blocker, repatriation restriction or regulatory upstreaming block is disclosed**; the credit agreement restricts **subsidiary** indebtedness, which runs the other way | Mapped and immaterial in size — there is no HoldCo-cannot-reach-the-cash problem of the usual kind. **What is not disclosed:** how much of the 945.0 offshore can be moved to the US, in what time, at what tax cost ("not practicable to estimate") — recorded as **not disclosed**, not assumed free. No cap applies: the HoldCo/OpCo structure **is** disclosed |
| **Contingent accelerants** | CoC puts / cross-default | **Change-of-control repurchase right at 101% of principal** on every Senior Notes series = **2,525.0 if all holders put**, of which only the **25.0 premium** is incremental to the debt `01` already counts — the real contingency is **timing** (immediate, not 2027–2034). **Cross-defaults to material indebtedness** and material-judgment defaults are events of default under the 2022 Credit Agreement; Senior Notes accelerate automatically on insolvency, otherwise on a **25% holder** demand. Revolver pricing is **rating-determined**, and the only rating in the pool is a **tier-5 vendor S&P BBB indication with no agency rationale or outlook** | Fast-failure paths exist and are disclosed. **No equity cure right** means a covenant breach needs a **lender waiver or amendment**, and a downgrade raises the cost of the one facility the company would lean on in a closed market — by an amount **not disclosed** |

**No cap binds any score in §1.** The limitations that do bind **confidence** rather than scores are: the **UNCHECKED numeric-reading status** (not verified clean); the **absence of a company-computed Leverage Ratio or compliance certificate**, which makes every covenant figure the engine's own calculation; the **conformed-redline ambiguity** in the credit-agreement exhibit, resolved from the FY26 10-K's own narrative; the **unquantified litigation maximum**; the **undisclosed peak working-capital need** and **undisclosed revolver commitment / letter-of-credit fees**, which make every liquidity figure an upper bound; and the **absence of any TTWO material-event (8-K) feed** (the Key Developments export in the pool resolves to a different issuer, Alphabet Inc.) plus an **empty DEF 14A stub**, so anything disclosed after **2026-11-09** is invisible here.

## 5. Survival Summary

**(a) How levered, and which way it is moving.** Moderately levered, and the direction has reversed twice for different reasons. Net leverage fell from **5.81x (FY2025) to 1.23x (FY2026)** and has since risen to **1.89x–2.02x** — but the fall was bought with **shareholders' money, not operating cash** (1,150.0 of notes repaid using proceeds from a May 2025 equity issuance that lifted the share count 61.7% since 2022, with zero buybacks in FY2024–FY2026), and the rise since is **pure cash burn with no new borrowing**, reconciled to the dollar. Gross leverage is **3.72x** on the company-defined base (4.30x including operating leases). The number that matters most is neither of those: on the **cash-backed** base of 373.2 the company is at **3.67x today**, because it spends roughly 1.7x as much cash on games (716.3 over the LTM) as it charges in amortisation (411.9), and EBITDA converted to operating cash at only **50.6%**.

**(b) Is the near-term wall self-funded or refinancing-dependent?** Self-funded for twelve months, refinancing-or-launch-dependent for twenty-four. The 629.4 due inside twelve months is covered **1.83x by cash & equivalents alone** and nothing breaks under a modelled 12-month market closure at any haircut tested. The 1,429.4 due inside twenty-four months is covered **1.17x by cash plus liquid bank deposits with no market access** and **1.87x** including 997.9 of committed, non-borrowing-base revolver availability — so it is payable without an open bond market, but **paying it leaves about 244.2 on the balance sheet**, and replenishing that rests on guided FY2027 operating cash flow of **over 1,000.0** after a first half that printed **(173.5)**, i.e. **over 1,173.5 in six months against a best-ever full year of 434.1**. There is **no announced issuance and no announced asset sale in this pool, and none is assumed**.

**(c) Runway, and how close the tightest covenant is.** The conservative gross-obligations runway is **31.0 months** (19.4 months on in-hand liquidity before the revolver and before any cash generation), and it does not fall below the 12-month window in any case this evidence supports — including the company's own **worst audited year** of cash generation (37.1 months) and a **zero-mitigation bound** annualising the latest half-year (23.9 months). The single maintenance covenant in the whole structure — a **3.75:1.00 maximum Leverage Ratio**, with **no springing trigger, no minimum-liquidity test, no interest-coverage test and no equity cure right** — sits at **1.07x (+71.4%)** on full contractual cash netting and **1.73x (+53.8%)** on the conservative cash-only anchor. Two dated, mechanical tightenings arrive before any trading deteriorates: the 29.4 Zynga convertible repays **2026-12-15** and the netting allowance steps down **2,000.0 → 1,500.0 on 2026-12-31**, together costing about **4.8 percentage points** of headroom from the quarter ending 2027-03-31 — visible only in the credit-agreement exhibit, in neither filing's narrative.

**(d) Where it first fails, and is a normal recession survivable?** **The covenant fails first, not the cash box — and on the conservative anchor it fails at about a 46% EBITDA decline** (45.6% joint cash-and-EBITDA stress with cash-only netting; 59.9% on full netting; 71.4% only if cash is frozen, which this covenant's own arithmetic forbids, because its numerator rises as cash drains while its denominator falls). **A normal recession is survivable without an equity raise, an asset sale or a waiver:** at **−30%** the structure needs a 37.2 revolver draw by the 24-month mark, and at **−40%** a 172.7 draw — both well inside the 693.3 the covenant permits — with committed liquidity still showing a 1,833.7 twelve-month surplus, covenant headroom of +40.6% on full netting, and interest covered 3.00x. **A −60% decline is not survivable unaided:** the covenant breaches at the 12-month quarter-end on **both** netting cases, and the 443.7 draw the 24-month gap needs **exceeds the 95.4 the covenant permits**, so a **lender waiver or amendment** becomes necessary with no equity cure available. Two cases in between deserve naming rather than averaging: the **own-history −43.4%** scenario (FY2022 peak 671.0 to FY2025 trough 379.5) leaves only **+4.7%** of headroom on the conservative anchor, and **−40% plus a repeat of the LTM 225.0 deferred-revenue outflow breaches it outright (−1.1%)**. First *visible* strain is far earlier: a **27.3%** decline empties cash plus deposits over 24 months and converts the funding plan from a balance-sheet fact into a covenant-conditional one. Every one of those figures is a **zero-mitigation bound**; at the **measured** realised cost response of +178.5 a year, a −40% shock becomes a **−13.7%** effective shock, the 24-month gap disappears, and headroom stays above +66%.

## 6. What Would Change The Solvency Verdict?

| Current Verdict | What Would Strengthen It | What Would Weaken It | Data Needed |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Adequate** | Guided FY2027 operating cash flow of **over 1,000.0** actually landing (which needs **over 1,173.5** in the six months to 2027-03-31) and rebuilding in-hand liquidity above the 1,673.6 at 2026-09-30 → the 24-month wall stops being launch-dependent and net leverage moves toward the guided **1.30x**. · Pre-funding or terming out the **600.0 due 2027-04-14** and/or the **800.0 due 2028-03-28**, which would cut the 56.5% 24-month concentration and lift the 2.93-year weighted-average maturity. · A **company-computed Leverage Ratio** in a compliance certificate confirming the engine's 1.07x — turning covenant headroom from a calculation into a reported figure. · A rating-agency rationale affirming investment grade, which also fixes the rating-determined revolver spread. · Sustained CFO/EBITDA back above 70% (it was 79.2% in FY2026, 50.6% over the LTM) | A GTA VI cycle that does not convert: another negative-FCF half-year would drain the in-hand pool toward the **27.3%** break-point and force a revolver draw. · Any **EBITDA decline toward 45%** with cash leaving at the same time — the conservative covenant anchor breaches there, and there is **no equity cure right**. · A **debt-funded acquisition** (the contract anticipates one: a Qualified Acquisition lifts the ceiling to 4.25x for four quarters, which buys room rather than removing it) or a resumption of buybacks against the 10.0 shares still authorised. · A **downgrade** below the vendor-indicated BBB, which raises the revolver's rate-determined spread by an undisclosed amount. · Crystallisation of the **274.5** tax file, or a litigation loss above an accrual whose maximum is **not disclosed**. · Confirmation that the **524.2 of bank time deposits does NOT qualify as "Permitted Investments"** — that alone moves the covenant anchor from +71.4% to +53.8% and the breach point from 59.9% to 45.6% | **(1) The quarterly covenant compliance certificate** under §5.01(c), showing the company's own computed Leverage Ratio and Consolidated Adjusted EBITDA — the single highest-value item, and the one that converts every covenant figure here from an engine calculation into a company-reported, auditor-adjacent number. **(2)** The identity and maturity of the banks holding the 524.2 of time deposits, against the contract's two-year and $250,000,000-capital tests. **(3)** The Q3 FY2027 10-Q (first reported period containing the GTA VI launch) with its cash-flow statement and refreshed maturity table. **(4)** A current S&P / Moody's / Fitch rationale and outlook. **(5)** A clean (non-redline) copy of Amendment No. 3 to the 2022 Credit Agreement, to remove the conformed-redline ambiguity on §6.05. **(6)** A quantified litigation maximum or a named material matter. **(7)** The split of maintenance versus growth capex, and the peak intra-quarter working-capital need |

## 6A. Survival Playbook (non-speculative levers)

Only levers with evidence in this pool. Each is a disclosed fact or a completed action, not a plan attributed to management.

- **Refi actions already taken (completed, committed).** **Amendment No. 3 to the 2022 Credit Agreement, dated 2025-05-19**, raised the revolver commitment from **750.0 to 1,000.0** and extended maturity to **2030-05-19**, with two one-year extension options — a completed refinancing of the facility itself [`FY26 10-K, Note 11 (Debt)`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt)`]. Recent debt activity has otherwise been **repayment, not refinancing**: 600.0 of 2025 Notes repaid 2025-04-14 and 550.0 of 2026 Notes repaid 2026-03-28.
- **Equity-funded deleveraging — demonstrated, not hypothetical.** The **May 2025 issuance of 5.5m shares at $225.00 for net proceeds of 1,192.8** was used to repay 1,150.0 of notes [`FY26 10-K, Note 12`; `01` §6]. The company has shown it can and will fund repayment from the equity market. Note the cost: the share count rose **61.7%** from 115.8m (2022-03-31) to 187.2m.
- **Covenant-relief precedent — the strongest evidenced lever.** The lender group has **already granted covenant relief and amendment**: the superseded §6.05 schedule ran at **4.75x (Jun-2024) / 5.75x (Sep-2024) / 5.50x (Dec-2024) / 4.25x (Mar & Jun-2025) / 4.00x (Sep-2025)** before stepping to 3.75x, and the agreement has been amended at least three times [`FY25 10-K, Exhibit 10.41`; `04` §2]. That is evidence a waiver or amendment has been obtainable before — which matters, because **there is no equity cure right**, so a waiver is the only route through a breach.
- **Dividend / buyback suspension ability — effectively already suspended, and contractually unconstrained.** **No dividend is paid or declared** in FY2024, FY2025, FY2026 or H1 FY27, and **no shares were repurchased** in any of those periods. A buyback authorisation exists (**10.0 shares still available** of 21.7 authorised) but the company states the programme "does not require us to repurchase shares and may be suspended or discontinued at any time for any reason" [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Item 2 MD&A`]. So **zero** committed shareholder returns enter any obligation figure — there is nothing to cut, which is itself a lever already pulled.
- **Uncommitted capacity available but excluded by rule — 494.0.** A **215.0 uncommitted** accounts-receivable purchase facility with Wells Fargo (entered 2025-05-19, priced at SOFR + 0.90–0.95%, **nil used** in FY2026 or H1 FY27), plus an **uncommitted** incremental revolver basket of the greater of 250.0 and 35.0% of Consolidated Adjusted EBITDA (**279.0** on `04`'s build). Both are **excluded from every liquidity figure** per MODULE_RULES Core Principle 4 — an uncommitted line can be pulled exactly when it is needed — but the company does list the receivables facility among the levers it may use "depending on cash flow needs and market conditions".
- **Capex flexibility — partially evidenced, not quantified.** Guided FY2027 capex is **290.0**, and the company does **not** split maintenance from growth anywhere in the pool, so a maintenance floor cannot be stated. What *is* evidenced: H1 FY27's 163.3 of fixed-asset purchases included buying the previously-leased London office building (capitalised cost **91.8**, completed 2026-08-10) — a discretionary, non-recurring item. `03` deliberately charged the **whole 290.0** as if unavoidable, which **overstates** the requirement.
- **Measured cost response — 178.5 a year, from the company's own numbers.** A **72.0** operating-expense reduction the company put into its own FY2027 guidance in one quarter's update, plus **106.5** from a realised **−160 bp** product-cost ratio improvement across FY2026 (14.6% → 13.0% of revenue) on FY2026 revenue of 6,656.4 [`06` §2]. **Two qualifiers travel with it:** the 72.0 was measured between two **guidance** tables, not two realised prints, and part was attributed to "timing"; the 106.5 was realised in a **rising**-revenue year through platform mix and a direct-to-consumer channel, so it is not proof the same move is available in a falling one.
- **Asset sales — no evidenced lever.** **None announced or authorised in the data pool, and none assumed.** Divestiture proceeds were **nil in FY2026** (32.7 in FY2025); the 2026-11 subsequent-events note discloses only "a reorganization involving certain intangible assets to simplify our global operating structure", with **no consideration and no proceeds** [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 16`].
- **Repatriating the 945.0 of offshore cash — not an evidenced lever.** No legal barrier, dividend blocker or regulatory restriction is disclosed, but **no timetable and no tax cost is disclosed either** — the company says the tax liability on remittance is "not practicable to estimate" [`FY26 10-K, Note 15`]. Recorded as a timing-and-tax question, not a usable lever and not netted out.

---

## 7. Note To The Final Synthesizer

- **Leverage, direction, both bases.** **Gross debt 2,519.8** (borrowings at carrying value; face 2,529.8; **2,911.4 including operating leases** — US GAAP keeps them off the debt line) at **3.72x** EBITDA. **Net debt 1,370.4 on the strict §15 basis — the module's canonical figure — at 2.02x**; broad basis 846.2 at 1.25x; vendor 1,237.8 (broad **and** lease-inclusive) at 1.71x. **Direction: halved then partly rebounded** — 5.81x → 1.23x → 1.89x — with the fall bought by an **equity issuance, not operating cash**, and the rebound caused by **cash burn, not new borrowing**. The multiple you quote depends entirely on the base: **2.42x mid-cycle is the central estimate, 1.30x on company guidance is a floor not a forecast, and 3.67x on cash-backed EBITDA is the cash-truthful figure**. **There is no GAAP EBITDA** — the GAAP operating result is a **loss** of (180.8) LTM — so every multiple is **non-GAAP**, and the canonical 677.6 is **partly engine-computed**. Do not headline a single multiple without its base and cycle label.
- **The wall, and whether refinancing is secured.** **56.5% of principal (1,429.4) within 24 months; 24.9% (629.4) within 12; weighted-average maturity 2.93 years** — not laddered. **Refinancing is NOT secured**: there is no announced or committed issuance and no announced asset sale in this pool. It is **pre-funded instead**: 24-month cover of **1.17x** on cash plus liquid deposits with no market access, **1.87x** including the revolver. The fiscal-2028 "cliff" of 1,400.0 is **two payments 11.5 months apart** (600.0 on 2027-04-14, 800.0 on 2028-03-28) — carry both readings. Refi step-up roughly **+90 to +145 bps** over a 4.59% coupon, the upper leg being an **indicative, unverified, mismatched-tenor bound**.
- **The runway and what it depends on.** **31.0 months** on the conservative gross-obligations basis; **19.4 months in-hand** before the revolver and before any cash generation; **24 to 57 months** across the net-of-FCF cases; **no finite runway** if FY2027 guidance lands. **Zero of the first 19.4 months needs free cash flow to appear.** It depends on three things in sequence: time (the in-hand cash box), then the revolver staying available (which needs the 3.75x covenant to hold), then Grand Theft Auto VI converting into cash. **82.2% of the cash (945.0) is offshore with an unquantified repatriation tax**, and the **peak intra-quarter working-capital need is not disclosed**, so treat every runway figure as the **upper** end of its case.
- **Cash-finance-cost bridge — carry the basis, count the payment once.** The chosen FCF is **reported §15 FCF = CFO − total capex** (**no adjusted measure and no proxy exists in this pool**). Classification was **established by direct read**, not assumed: cash interest (**153.0** FY2026; **57.4** in H1 FY27) and operating-lease payments (**82.4** FY2026) are **all inside CFO** — so **cash finance costs absent from the chosen FCF = 0.0**, nothing is re-added on the net-of-FCF basis, and the **114.8** forward coupon is used *only* on the gross-obligations basis. **Do not drop the "reported" qualifier and do not label this accounting FCF as "cash after interest" without that bridge** — here the bridge happens to confirm the source *has* deducted the payment, which is why the qualifier is safe to state rather than safe to omit. **Unresolved limits that must travel:** no supplemental "interest paid" line in the 10-Q (closed by arithmetic, 57.4 = half of 114.8); **revolver commitment and letter-of-credit fees not disclosed and not assumed zero**; capitalised interest not disclosed either way.
- **The tightest covenant and its headroom.** **Maximum Leverage Ratio 3.75 : 1.00**, §6.05 of the 2022 Credit Agreement (as amended 2025-05-19), tested every fiscal quarter, **permanently active, not springing** — and the **only** maintenance financial covenant in the entire structure (no interest-coverage, no minimum-liquidity, no net-worth test; the Senior Notes indenture has none). **Actual 1.07x → +71.4%** on full contractual cash netting; **1.73x → +53.8%** on the conservative cash-only-netting anchor. **Covenants are DISCLOSED and numeric — but the company's own computed ratio is not: every covenant figure here is the engine's own calculation on the contract's definitions, and the §5.01(c) compliance certificate is not in the pool.** Covenant EBITDA of 797.2 is a deliberate **floor**, so the error runs toward **too little** headroom. **No equity cure right** — a breach needs a lender waiver or amendment. **Dated tightening: the netting allowance steps down 2,000.0 → 1,500.0 on 2026-12-31**, costing about **4.8 percentage points** of headroom from the quarter ending 2027-03-31, disclosed in neither filing's narrative.
- **Largest live off-balance-sheet / contingent exposure.** **740.1 of binding, dated contractual commitments** (software development and licensing 175.8 + marketing 206.9 + purchase obligations 357.4), with **323.7 due inside twelve months** — a floor on cash outflow that **does not shrink when net bookings fall**. Beside it, the **274.5 of gross unrecognized tax benefits** (including 55.6 of accrued interest and penalties) with **eight fiscal years, FY2018–FY2025, currently under examination** and the company stating amounts paid "may differ materially from the amounts accrued". Total maximum exposure **1,567.8 = 44.7% of equity**, **2.19x recognized** (3.70x excluding already-recognized leases) — **and that total is a FLOOR, not a ceiling**, because five items have **no quantified maximum** (litigation above the accrual, tax above 274.5, the **redacted** Microsoft platform defence-of-claims cap, repatriation tax on 945.0 offshore, surety bonds not disclosed either way).
- **`RF-OBS-001 (contingent-liability spike)` — FIRED by `05` and propagated here as a standalone line.** Thresholds trip mechanically (maximum ÷ equity **44.7%** against a 15% rule of thumb; maximum ÷ recognized **3.70x** against a 3x rule of thumb once already-recognized leases come out of both sides) and the underlying matters are **live, not dormant** on the company's own language ("currently examining" eight tax years; "reasonably possible" excess litigation loss; 323.7 of commitments due within twelve months). **It is NOT a finding of a hidden or undisclosed liability** — everything is disclosed, dated and quantified except the five items named above, and the biggest component is ordinary-course content and marketing spend rather than a claim against the company. This module's own solvency scores already absorbed it, **and it is still carried as a standalone tag** so the master synthesizer's cross-module forensic roll-up (synthesizer.md Pre-Write Gate step 4B; CLAUDE.md §13; eval check AQ) can read it — dropping a fired tag would let that compounding-mosaic check silently miss it (CLAUDE.md §11).
- **The stress break point — what fails first, and at what decline.** **A covenant fails first, not the cash box.** Conservative anchor (joint cash-and-EBITDA stress, cash-only netting): **45.6%**. Full netting: **59.9%**. Absolute pass-through variants: 68.2% / 51.9%. Static, cash frozen: 71.4% — **do not use it alone**, because this covenant's numerator **rises** as cash drains. **First visible strain at 27.3%** (in-hand cash plus deposits emptied over 24 months, forcing a revolver draw). **Committed liquidity is never exhausted by an EBITDA decline alone** inside either window (h = 310.6% / 100.9%). **−30% and −40% survive with no waiver, equity raise or asset sale** (37.2 and 172.7 revolver draws needed, both inside the 693.3 the covenant permits); **−60% does not** (covenant breaches at +12 months on both netting cases; the 443.7 draw needed exceeds the 95.4 permitted; waiver or amendment required, with no equity cure). **−40% plus a working-capital shock breaches the conservative anchor (−1.1%).** Every figure is a **zero-mitigation bound**; the **measured** realised-offset case (+178.5 a year, with both qualifiers attached) turns −40% into **−13.7%** effective. **These are levels, not probabilities — the master assigns probabilities, this module does not.**
- **Not net cash — the §24 Filter-3 credit does NOT apply.** TTWO carries net debt on **both** bases (strict 1,370.4; broad 846.2), so there is no counter-cyclical-optionality credit to award and no "lazy balance sheet" framing to reject. What the structure **does** earn is a positive read on its *terms*, and that should not be lost in the leverage number: **100% fixed-rate, 100% unsecured, no collateral, no borrowing base, no finance leases, no preferred, no defined-benefit deficit disclosed, exactly one maintenance covenant with no springing trigger, a committed facility to 2030-05-19, and zero committed shareholder returns.** A **+200 bp** rate shock costs **$0.0** on drawn debt, and the company's net interest exposure runs the **other** way (90.0 of LTM interest income), so falling rates are the adverse direction — do not restate that as "no rate risk".
- **Partial-data caps: NONE applied, and that is a positive finding, not an omission.** All five synthesis cap triggers and all six MODULE_RULES additional triggers are **N** (see §4), so **covenant headroom and downside resilience are both assessable and scored**, and overall usefulness is not capped at 70 or 75. What limits **confidence** rather than scores: the **UNCHECKED numeric-reading status (not verified clean)**; the **absence of any company-computed Leverage Ratio or compliance certificate**, which makes every covenant figure an engine calculation; the **conformed-redline ambiguity** in Exhibit 10.41 (resolved from the FY26 10-K's own narrative, with the resolution shown); the **unquantified litigation maximum**; the **undisclosed peak working-capital need and undisclosed facility fees** (so liquidity figures are upper bounds); and the **absence of any TTWO material-event (8-K) feed** — the Key Developments export in the pool resolves to **Alphabet Inc.**, a different issuer — plus an **empty DEF 14A stub**, so anything disclosed after **2026-11-09** is invisible. **No rating-agency rationale or outlook is in the pool**: the only rating read is a **tier-5 vendor S&P BBB indication** with vendor bottom-quartile credit-health scores against 25 peers — **do not infer a rating action, outlook or trajectory**, and note that the revolver's spread is **rating-determined** by an undisclosed step.
- **Biggest missing data point (one item).** **The quarterly covenant compliance certificate** required by §5.01(c), showing the company's own computed Leverage Ratio and Consolidated Adjusted EBITDA on the agreement's definitions. It is the single item that converts the entire covenant picture — 1.07x, +71.4% / +53.8%, and the 45.6%–59.9% break-points that follow from it — from the engine's own calculation into a company-reported, auditor-adjacent figure. (Second-best: a current S&P / Moody's / Fitch rationale and outlook, which also sets the revolver's rating-determined spread.)
- **Explicit handoff.** The master synthesizer's **"Balance Sheet and Survival Test" section should DEFER to this synthesis** rather than re-derive it. The verdict is **Adequate**; net leverage is **2.02x strict / 3.72x gross** on a **non-GAAP** base, **2.42x mid-cycle**, **3.67x cash-backed**; the runway is **31.0 months** conservative / **19.4 in-hand**; the tightest (and only) covenant is a **3.75x leverage ceiling at 1.07x / +71.4%**, or **+53.8%** on the conservative anchor; and the structure **first breaks at a covenant at roughly a 46% EBITDA decline**, with first visible strain at **27.3%** and an unaided failure at **−60%**. **Those break points are the inputs for the master's downside scenario and risk register — the master assigns the probabilities, this module does not.** Carry `RF-OBS-001` into the cross-module forensic roll-up as a standalone tag. Carry the **UNCHECKED numeric-reading status** forward as an unresolved source-check obligation, not as a clean bill. And note for §14 (thesis type): on this module's evidence alone, **the 24-month funding plan reduces to one unreported product cycle** — the survival read is contingent on a product event, not on a macro or commodity variable.

---

## 8. Simple Summary

- **Debt:** about **$2.52bn** of borrowings, all unsecured and all at fixed rates, against **$1.15bn** of cash. That is **$1.37bn of net debt** — **2.0 times** a trailing profit measure the company defines itself (there is no standard one, because the accounting result is a loss), or **2.4 times** on its own five-year average, or **3.7 times** if you charge the cash it really spends making games. Gross debt is **3.7 times** the same profit measure.
- **The wall:** **$629m** due in the next twelve months (the first piece, $29m, on **2026-12-15**; then **$600m on 2027-04-14**) and **$1.43bn — 56.5% of all the debt — inside two years**, with the big one, **$800m, on 2028-03-28**. Cash and bank deposits cover the twelve-month piece **1.8 times** and the two-year piece **1.2 times** with no help from any lender.
- **Runway:** about **31 months** on the cautious count, **19 months** on cash and deposits alone before touching the credit line. It does not drop below a year in any case the evidence supports.
- **The one covenant:** the only promise to lenders that can be broken is a ceiling of **3.75 times leverage**, and the company sits at about **1.07 times** (or **1.73 times** on the stricter reading of what cash counts). So there is room — but it is **our calculation, not the company's**, because the company never publishes the number, and there is **no right to fix a breach by raising equity**. A dated technical tightening on **2026-12-31** quietly eats about **5 percentage points** of that room.
- **Biggest off-balance-sheet item:** **$740m** of signed, dated promises to game developers, licensors, sports leagues and suppliers — **$324m of it due within a year** — which do **not** shrink if sales fall. Behind that, **$275m of disputed tax** with **eight years of returns under examination**. The full maximum, **$1.57bn**, is **44.7% of shareholders' equity** — and it is a floor, because five items have **no stated ceiling**, including a litigation maximum the company never gives.
- **Does it survive a 30–60% profit drop?** **A 30% and a 40% drop: yes**, with no rescue financing, no asset sale and no lender permission needed — it would borrow **$37m** or **$173m** on its credit line, well inside what the covenant allows. **A 60% drop: no** — the covenant breaks within a year and the borrowing it would need is more than the covenant permits, so it would need lenders to agree to a waiver. The break point is about a **46% drop** on the cautious reading, and the first real strain shows at about **27%**.
- **Ratings and key gaps:** there is **no rating-agency report in the pool** — only a data-vendor note of **S&P BBB** plus vendor scores putting the company in the **bottom quarter of 25 similar companies**, so no rating view is formed here. The key gap is the **lender compliance certificate**: without it, every covenant number in this report is ours, not the company's. There is also **no company news feed after 2026-11-09**, so anything announced since is invisible.
- **Useful for the master synthesizer?** **Yes — highly.** All six analyses ran on primary filings, **no data cap binds any score**, and the break points are explicit and reproducible. Use the leverage range, the two-year wall, the runway, the covenant headroom and the ~46% break point as given; add the probabilities elsewhere.

**Evidence binding (MODULE_PIPELINE Step 1.5).** The complete frozen quartet was present, so this agent ran in frozen-evidence mode: `NOSTRA_FROZEN_EVIDENCE_ROOT`, `NOSTRA_FROZEN_POOL_DATA_PATH`, `NOSTRA_FROZEN_POOL_OUT_DIR`, `NOSTRA_FROZEN_POOL_GENERATION` all resolved. `DATA_PATH` = the frozen evidence root only; `GENERATION_ROOT` = `.extract-generations/0af638492ecb0521db2676c79d75e6940c7227bb9f192567527b8a57eb43203d`. Every manifest `extract` reference carries exactly that prefix (checked: 0 prefix violations across 30 sources and 62 tabs). `extract_pool.py` was NOT run, no live `data/TTWO/` read was attempted, and `analyses/TTWO_2026-11-19/_pool_extracts/` was not opened. `data/TTWO/` below is a citation label only.

**Pool totals from the exact generation manifest:** 30 sources · 19 workbooks · 62 tabs · 73 extracts written · **0 failures**. No source carries `status` of `fail`, `fallback-text`, `missing-dependency`, or `gdrive-pointer`.

**Numeric-reading check status — UNCHECKED, not verified clean.** All 30 manifest sources carry **no `numeric_reading_check` field at all**. Absence of a warning is therefore not evidence of accurate numeric extraction; the check simply did not run on this generation. No labelled extractor annotation or warning token appears anywhere in `GENERATION_ROOT/corpus.txt` (searched for annotation/warning markers: none found), so there is **no specific flagged amount** to quarantine and nothing to mark "not proven" on reading-warning grounds. The duty this transfers downstream: every material solvency amount (debt principal by instrument, cash, maturities by year, interest expense, CFO/capex, lease liabilities, revolver availability, covenant threshold) must be read directly from the frozen filing extract that reports it — `10K_2025_26_0001628280-26-037434.txt`, `10Q_2Q_0001628280-26-072254.txt`, `10K_2024_25_0001628280-25-026694.txt` — and the Capital IQ workbook tabs must be used as a vendor **read to reconcile against**, never as the authority for a filing's own number (§5). All four filings extracted as native PDF text (no OCR path, 274 / 190 / 50 / 93-page native coverage), so a direct verification read is available inside the frozen capability for every affected amount. This status caps nothing on its own and does not change the sufficiency verdict; it is carried forward as a standing verification obligation for agents 01–06 and 99.

It is a wrong-entity export with zero TTWO solvency content. There is no TTWO Key Developments export, so TTWO material-event / rating-action news flow is absent from the pool (noted as a limitation, not a sufficiency gap for this module).

**"Last Modified" column.** Every file in a frozen evidence snapshot carries the same snapshot copy timestamp (2026-11-20 11:26), which is the freeze time, not a period. Per fix F23 the modified date is therefore recorded as non-informative throughout, and **every period below is parsed from INSIDE the document** ("For the fiscal year ended…", "For the quarterly period ended…", the vendor tab's own `Source:` / `As of` / period-column headers).

## 5. Partial-Data Flags

| Missing Data | Applies? (Y/N) | Affected Agents | Cap Applied |
|---|---|---|---|
| No debt maturity schedule | **N** | 02, 06 | None — year-by-year schedule as of 2026-09-30 is in `10Q_2Q`, plus per-instrument maturity dates in CIQ Capital-Structure-Details |
| No covenant disclosure | **N** | 04, 06 | None — the numeric maximum-Leverage-Ratio schedule and the full covenant-EBITDA definition are in the pool (FY25 10-K Exhibit 10.41 §6.05). See the limitation note below: the company does not publish its computed Leverage Ratio or a compliance certificate, so `04` must compute headroom itself from the agreement's own definitions and label that calculation |
| No cash flow statement | **N** | 03, 04, 06 | None — audited FY2026 statement, H1 FY27 condensed statement, and CIQ LTM-to-Sep-30-2026 column all present |
| No undrawn-facility disclosure | **N** | 03 | None — committed $1,000.0 revolver with stated availability $997.9 at 2026-09-30 and $2.1 of outstanding letters of credit |
| No interest-expense detail | **N** | 04 | None — interest expense by year plus per-instrument coupons disclosed |
| No EBITDA base | **N** | 06 | None — LTM EBITDA $724.9m (CIQ, LTM Sep-30-2026) plus a ten-year own-history series and forward consensus |

**No partial-data cap and no score cap binds.** The checked caps that could have applied — maturity schedule, covenant disclosure, cash flow statement, interim data, undrawn facilities, revolver availability, covenant-EBITDA addbacks, HoldCo/OpCo disclosure, EBITDA base, off-balance-sheet exposures — are each satisfied by a named source above.

**Limitations that do NOT trigger a cap but must travel downstream (carry these qualifiers at every layer, §3):**
1. **Numeric-reading check is UNCHECKED on all 30 sources** (no `numeric_reading_check` field anywhere; no corpus warning annotations). Not verified clean. Every material amount must be verified by a direct read of the frozen filing extract that reports it; CIQ workbook figures are a vendor read to reconcile against, never a substitute for the filing's own number (§5). Affected inputs: all debt principals, cash, maturities-by-year, interest expense, CFO/capex, lease liabilities, revolver availability, covenant threshold. Needed source check: read the figure in `10K_2025_26…txt` / `10Q_2Q…txt` / `10K_2024_25…txt` within the frozen capability before publishing it.
2. **Net-debt basis.** `ciq_facts.json` reports net debt **$1,237.8m** against total debt **$2,911.4m** at FQ2 Sep-30-2026 — the source_ref itself warns this is a **CIQ vendor basis that may net short-term/liquid investments** (a broad basis, §15). `01` must designate the **strict** basis (total debt − cash & equivalents) as the module's canonical figure, label any broad figure as broad, and reconcile the ~$1.67bn implied netting. Also note CIQ's $2,911.4m total debt is larger than the filings' $2,529.8m face value of borrowings, consistent with the vendor including the $440.3m operating-lease liability — `01` must state which view it is using.
3. **Covenant compliance is not stated numerically.** Neither 10-K nor 10-Q discloses the computed Leverage Ratio or an explicit "we were in compliance" line, and no compliance certificate is in the pool. The threshold (3.75x, or 4.25x in a post-Qualified-Acquisition window) and the definitions are available, so headroom IS computable — but it will be the engine's own calculation on covenant definitions, not a company-reported figure, and `04` must label it that way and state the $1bn indebtedness-netting allowance it relies on.
4. **Litigation maximum exposure is not quantified** — Note 14 gives accrued-amount language and a "not material" assertion, not a numeric ceiling. `05` records the commitments total ($740.1m) as quantified and the litigation maximum as not disclosed (per MODULE_RULES: "Not disclosed in the data pool"), without inventing a number.
5. **No rating-agency report.** The S&P BBB indication comes from a tier-5 vendor panel (CIQ Credit Health Panel), not a rating action. `99` must note the absence and must not infer an outlook or rating trajectory.
6. **Nearest maturity is imminent.** CIQ Capital-Structure-Details dates the $30m (filings: $29.4m) 2026 Convertible Notes at **2026-12-15**, under a month from this triage date — `02` and `03` should treat it as a current-period item, not a future maturity.
7. **No TTWO material-event (8-K / Key Developments) feed in the pool**, and the DEF 14A export is an empty stub. Rating changes, covenant amendments, or debt issuances after 2026-11-09 would not be visible here.
8. **Vendor maturity-wall fact is dated earlier than the filing.** `ciq_facts.json` debt_maturity_wall (WAM ~3.4y, 0 floating / 2,970 fixed) is computed from the **FY2026 (Mar-31-2026)** as-reported block; the `10Q_2Q` table at 2026-09-30 is the fresher authority for the wall.

---

## 6. Sufficiency Verdict

- **Verdict:** **Sufficient**
- **Reason:** A recent balance sheet (2026-09-30 10-Q), a full debt note with amounts by instrument and a year-by-year maturity schedule, and audited plus interim cash flow statements are all present — and the pool additionally carries the full credit-agreement text with the numeric maximum-leverage covenant, its covenant-EBITDA definition, stated revolver availability, interest-expense detail, lease schedules, and the commitments and contingencies note, so leverage, liquidity, coverage, covenant headroom, and a downside stress test can all be built from primary filings.
- **Sections that can run:** capital structure and leverage · maturity wall and refinancing · liquidity runway · coverage and covenants · off-balance-sheet and contingencies · downside stress test — **all six**, plus the synthesis.
- **Active partial-data caps:** none (verdict is Sufficient; see the eight limitation notes in §5 that travel downstream as qualifiers, not caps).
- **Critical missing items:** none for solvency assessment. The only genuinely absent solvency-adjacent documents are a rating-agency rationale/outlook and a TTWO material-event feed, neither of which is required by the sufficiency rule.
- **Single highest-value missing document:** a **quarterly covenant compliance certificate** showing the company's own computed Leverage Ratio and Consolidated Adjusted EBITDA as defined in the 2022 Credit Agreement — it is the one item that would turn `04`'s headroom from an engine calculation on contract definitions into a company-reported, auditor-adjacent figure. (Second-best: a current S&P/Moody's rating rationale, which also sets the revolver's pricing step.)

**Company:** Take-Two Interactive Software, Inc. (NasdaqGS:TTWO) · **Date:** 2026-11-19
**Reporting currency:** **USD, in millions** unless stated. **Reporting standard: US GAAP.** Fiscal year ends **31 March** (so "FY2026" = year ended 2026-03-31, and the latest balance sheet is the quarter ended **2026-09-30**, which is Q2 of FY2027).
**Filing regime:** US SEC — Form 10-K (annual), Form 10-Q (quarterly).

**Standing verification note carried forward from `00_solvency-data-triage.md` §5.** All 30 manifest sources in this frozen generation carry **no `numeric_reading_check` field at all** — they are **unchecked, not verified clean** — and no labelled extractor warning annotation appears in `GENERATION_ROOT/corpus.txt`, so there is no specific flagged amount to quarantine. The duty that transfers: **every material amount in this report was read directly from the frozen filing extract that reports it** — `10Q_2Q_0001628280-26-072254.txt` (Q2 FY27 10-Q), `10K_2025_26_0001628280-26-037434.txt` (FY26 10-K), `10K_2024_25_0001628280-25-026694.txt` (FY25 10-K) — and the Capital IQ workbook tabs are used only as a vendor **read to reconcile against**, never as the authority for a filing's own number (CLAUDE.md §5). | — 2028 Notes | 800.0 face (fair value 796.5) | Take-Two (parent) | No | Senior unsecured | None | 2028-03-28 | Fixed 4.95% | `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt)` |
| — 2029 Notes | 300.0 face (fair value 298.3) | Take-Two (parent) | No | Senior unsecured | None | 2029-06-12 | Fixed 5.40% | `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt)` |
| — 2032 Notes | 500.0 face (fair value 454.8) | Take-Two (parent) | No | Senior unsecured | None | 2032-04-14 | Fixed 4.00% | `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt)` |
| — 2034 Notes | 300.0 face (fair value 289.6) | Take-Two (parent) | No | Senior unsecured | None | 2034-06-12 | Fixed 5.60% | `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt)` |
| — less unamortized discount & issuance cost | (9.4) | — | — | — | — | — | — | `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt)` |
| **Revolver (drawn)** | **Nil drawn** on a **$1,000.0 committed** unsecured five-year revolving facility; **$997.9 available**; $2.1 of letters of credit outstanding | Take-Two (Borrower under the 2022 Credit Agreement) | **No** — unsecured | Senior unsecured | None | **2030-05-19**, with two one-year extension options | **Floating** — 0.000–0.625% over an alternate base rate (7.00% at 2026-09-30) **or** 1.000–1.625% over SOFR (~3.91% at 2026-09-30); the spread is **set by the Company's credit rating** | `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt) — Credit Agreement`; `FY26 10-K, Note 11 (Debt) — Credit Agreement` |
| **Finance / capital leases** | **None.** The phrase "finance lease" does not appear anywhere in the FY26 10-K (0 occurrences); all leases are **operating** leases | n/a | n/a | n/a | n/a | n/a | n/a | Direct read of `FY26 10-K` (frozen extract) |
| **Total gross debt — face value of borrowings** | **2,529.8** | — | — | — | — | — | — | `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt)` maturities table: Total 2,529.4 + fair-value adjustments 0.4 = **Total face value 2,529.8** |
| **Total gross debt — balance-sheet carrying value** | **2,519.8** | — | — | — | — | — | — | 629.2 + 1,890.6, both read off the `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Condensed Consolidated Balance Sheets` |

**Ties to the balance sheet.** Short-term debt, net $629.2 **plus** long-term debt, net $1,890.6 **equals $2,519.8**, which is the sum of the two debt lines printed on the Q2 FY27 10-Q balance sheet — no residual. The $10.0 gap to the $2,529.8 face value is the $9.4 + $0.6 of unamortized discount and issuance cost, plus the $0.4 fair-value adjustment on the convertible. Senior Notes principal alone is **$2,500.0** ("As of September 30, 2026, we had $2,500.0 of Senior Notes outstanding" — `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Item 2 MD&A`).

**Everything is unsecured and all borrowings are fixed-rate.** No collateral is pledged against any borrowing in either filing, and every note carries a fixed coupon; the only floating-rate instrument in the structure is the **undrawn** revolver. The vendor export agrees: "0 floating / 2,970 fixed" [`ciq_facts.json` → `debt_maturity_wall`, from `CIQ Financials → Capital Structure Details` — tier-5 vendor export]. Weighted-average coupon on the $2,500.0 of Senior Notes = (600×3.70 + 800×4.95 + 300×5.40 + 500×4.00 + 300×5.60) ÷ 2,500 = 11,480 ÷ 2,500 = **4.59%** (my arithmetic from the coupons in `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10`).

**One vendor-convention difference to note, not a conflict.** `CIQ Financials_Quarterly → Capital Structure Details` lists "Operating Lease Liabilities 440.3, Secured: Yes" alongside the notes [vendor export, as-reported FY2026 block, `Source: Q2 2027 filed Nov-09-2026`]. That is the vendor treating the leased asset as collateral for the lease; the **filings disclose no secured borrowings**. The $440.3 is also the **2026-03-31** lease liability, not the latest — at 2026-09-30 it is $391.6 (see §2).

Minimum contractual commitments ($740.1 total at 2026-03-31) and contingencies belong to `05_off-balance-sheet-and-contingencies` and are not debt-stack items here.

## 4. Gross & Net Debt

As of **2026-09-30**, USD millions. Gross debt here is **borrowings at balance-sheet carrying value ($2,519.8)**; the face-value variant ($2,529.8) is shown alongside because it is the number the maturity schedule is built on.

| Metric | Value | Source |
|---|---:|---|
| **Gross debt — borrowings only, carrying value** | **2,519.8** | 629.2 + 1,890.6 [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Condensed Consolidated Balance Sheets`] |
| *Gross debt — borrowings only, face value (memo)* | *2,529.8* | `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt)` maturities table, "Total face value" |
| *Gross debt — borrowings PLUS operating-lease liabilities (the IFRS-16-equivalent view; memo)* | *2,911.4* | 2,519.8 + 391.6 lease liabilities [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Condensed Consolidated Balance Sheets`] — this is also exactly the vendor's "Total Debt" of 2,911.4 [`ciq_facts.json` → `total_debt_m`, `CIQ Financials → Balance Sheet` FQ2 Sep-30-2026] |
| − Cash & equivalents | (1,149.4) | `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Condensed Consolidated Balance Sheets` |
| **Net debt (strict, §15) — gross debt − cash & equivalents** | **1,370.4** | 2,519.8 − 1,149.4. **This is the module's canonical figure** (see §7). On face-value debt the strict figure is 1,380.4 |
| − Liquid short-term investments | (524.2) | `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 5 (Short-Term Investments)` |
| **Net debt (broad, incl. short-term investments)** | **846.2** | 1,370.4 − 524.2 |
| *Net debt (broad **and** lease-inclusive) — the vendor's basis* | *1,237.8* | `ciq_facts.json` → `net_debt_m` = 1,237.8 [`CIQ Financials → Balance Sheet` 'Net Debt', FQ2 Sep-30-2026 — the sidecar's own `source_ref` warns this is a CIQ vendor basis that may net short-term/liquid investments] |

**Reconciliation of the vendor's $1,237.8 to the strict $1,370.4 — it is a definitional difference, not a misread.** The ~$1.67bn of implied netting that the triage flagged resolves exactly, with no residual:

> strict 1,370.4 − short-term investments 524.2 + operating-lease liabilities 391.6 = **1,237.8** ✔

Equivalently, from the vendor side: (borrowings 2,519.8 + leases 391.6) − (cash 1,149.4 + short-term investments 524.2) = 2,911.4 − 1,673.6 = **1,237.8** ✔. So the vendor figure is **broad on the cash side AND lease-inclusive on the debt side** — two departures from §15 in the same number. It is reported above with both labels attached, as §15 requires, and it is **not** the canonical figure. `earnings/01_historical-financials.md` §1 reaches the identical reconciliation independently.

The strict basis is the §15 default and is the right default here for a second reason: $524.2m of the liquidity sits in bank time deposits with maturities over 90 days, which the company itself classifies outside cash equivalents.
