## 5. Leverage Ratios

### 5a. The EBITDA bases, stated before they are used

There is **no reported (GAAP) EBITDA** for this company, because US GAAP has no EBITDA line and TTWO's GAAP operating result is a **loss**:

| GAAP profit anchor | Value | Source |
|---|---:|---|
| Income (loss) from operations, FY2026 | **(104.2)** | `FY26 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Operations` |
| Loss from operations, six months to 2026-09-30 | **(153.0)** | `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Condensed Consolidated Statements of Operations` |
| Loss from operations, six months to 2025-09-30 | (76.4) | `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Condensed Consolidated Statements of Operations` |
| **GAAP operating loss, LTM to 2026-09-30** (engine bridge: −104.2 − (−76.4) + (−153.0)) | **(180.8)** | My arithmetic from the three filing figures above |

So any "debt ÷ reported GAAP operating profit" ratio has a **negative denominator and is not meaningful**. Every leverage multiple below therefore rests on a **non-GAAP EBITDA**, and that qualifier must travel with the number at every layer (§3).

| EBITDA base | Value | What it is, and how it was built |
|---|---:|---|
| **(A) Company-defined EBITDA, LTM to 2026-09-30** | **677.6** | **The company's own published non-GAAP definition:** "GAAP net income (loss) excluding interest income (expense), provision for (benefit from) income taxes, depreciation expense, and amortization and impairment of **acquired** intangibles" [`Q2 FY27 earnings release, 2026-11-09, Non-GAAP Financial Measure`]. Company-disclosed on that definition: **H1 FY27 (six months to 2026-09-30) = 259.2**; H1 FY26 = 342.2; Q1 FY27 = 167.0; Q2 FY27 = 92.2 [`Q2 FY27 earnings release, Reconciliation of GAAP to Non-GAAP Measure`; `Q1 FY27 earnings release, 2026-08-06, same table`]. The company does **not** publish a FY2026 full-year EBITDA in this pool, so **FY2026 = 760.6 is my own build on the company's stated definition, from the FY26 10-K's own lines**: net loss (298.2) + income taxes 100.4 + interest expense 151.4 − interest income (85.1) + depreciation 166.4 + amortization and impairment of intangibles 725.7 = **760.6** [`FY26 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Operations`; `FY26 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows`; `FY26 10-K, Note 17 (Interest and Other, Net)`; `FY26 10-K, Note 9` total intangible amortization 725.7]. **LTM bridge: 760.6 − 342.2 + 259.2 = 677.6.** Labelled **engine-computed**, not company-reported |
| **(B) Vendor EBITDA, LTM to 2026-09-30** | **724.9** | `ciq_facts.json` → `ltm_ebitda_m` = **724.9** [`CIQ Financials → Income Statement` 'EBITDA', LTM 12 months Sep-30-2026 — tier-5 vendor export]. My own read of that workbook tab matches 724.9 exactly, so there is **no misread to flag**. FY2026 on the same vendor basis = 788.5 |
| **(C) Mid-cycle / normalised EBITDA** | **565.3** | Five-year average of the company's own annual EBITDA on the vendor basis, FY2022–FY2026: (671.0 + 555.5 + 432.2 + 379.5 + 788.5) ÷ 5 = 2,826.7 ÷ 5 = **565.3** [`CIQ Financials_Annual → Income Statement`, EBITDA row — vendor export; my arithmetic]. Chosen over the ten-year average ($471.8m, computed in `business-model/07_business-quality.md §6`) because the pre-FY2022 years predate the Zynga acquisition and describe a company roughly half today's revenue |
| **(D) Company-guided FY2027 EBITDA — the coming peak (forward, not realised)** | **1,024 to 1,082** (midpoint **1,053**) | `Q2 FY27 earnings release, 2026-11-09, Outlook — Fiscal Year Ending March 31, 2027`, reconciled from guided net income $109–142 + taxes $86–111 + net interest expense $41 + depreciation $165 + amortization of acquired intangibles $623. **A company forecast, not a reported period** — use only as a labelled forward cross-check |

**(A) vs (B) — the reconciliation, and which one leads.** My filing-derived company-definition figure is **$47.3m (6.5%) below** the vendor's LTM EBITDA (677.6 vs 724.9), and $27.9m (3.5%) below it at FY2026 (760.6 vs 788.5). Both gaps run the same way. Per §4/§5 the **filing-derived figure (A) leads** for the headline, because it is built from the audited filing's own lines on the company's own published definition; the vendor figure (B) is cited for its own number and is **not overridden** — both are shown on every ratio below so the reader can see the spread. A separate vendor inconsistency, named: `CIQ Financials_Annual → Ratios` reports Total Debt/EBITDA of **3.639x** and Net Debt/EBITDA of **1.547x** for the LTM column, which implies a denominator of ~$799.9m, not $724.9m — at FY2026 the same tab's implied denominator is **exactly $868.1m, which is that tab's own EBITDA**R** line** (EBITDA before rent). So the vendor's Ratios tab is computed on **EBITDAR**, which is why it prints lower leverage. I use EBITDA, not EBITDAR, throughout.

**What neither EBITDA base adds back, and why that matters.** Both (A) and (B) add back amortization of **acquired** intangibles but **not** amortization of capitalized game-development costs ($412.8 in FY2026, $190.8 in H1 FY27) [`FY26 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Condensed Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows`]. That is the conservative treatment and it is the right one, because the company spent **$688.9m of cash on software development costs and licenses in FY2026** and $372.4m in H1 FY27 on the same line. A "standard" EBITDA that added **all** depreciation and amortization back to the FY2026 operating loss would read (104.2) + 166.4 + 725.7 + 412.8 = **$1,200.7m** and would cut gross leverage to 2.1x — that number is **not used here**, because it would add back an amortization charge matched by a larger recurring cash outflow.

### 5b. The ratios

Gross debt = **2,519.8** (borrowings, carrying value). Net debt = **1,370.4** (**strict §15 basis** — the canonical figure). Lease-inclusive variants shown in brackets where material. Higher = worse on every row.

| Ratio | On Reported EBITDA | On Adjusted EBITDA | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| **Gross debt / EBITDA** | **Not meaningful** — no GAAP EBITDA exists; the nearest reported measure is the **LTM GAAP operating loss of (180.8)**, a negative denominator | **3.72x** on (A) company-defined LTM EBITDA 677.6 · **3.48x** on (B) vendor LTM EBITDA 724.9 · [lease-inclusive: 4.30x on (A), 4.02x on (B)] | 2,519.8 ÷ 677.6 = 3.72x; 2,519.8 ÷ 724.9 = 3.48x; 2,911.4 ÷ 677.6 = 4.30x; 2,911.4 ÷ 724.9 = 4.02x. Inputs per §4 and §5a |
| **Net debt / EBITDA** (**strict** net debt 1,370.4) | **Not meaningful** (as above) | **2.02x** on (A) · **1.89x** on (B) · [lease-inclusive, strict cash: 2.60x on (A), 2.43x on (B)] | 1,370.4 ÷ 677.6 = 2.02x; 1,370.4 ÷ 724.9 = 1.89x |
| *Net debt / EBITDA on the **broad** basis (846.2) — shown for completeness, not canonical* | *Not meaningful* | *1.25x on (A) · 1.17x on (B)*. The vendor's own broad-**and**-lease-inclusive figure: **1.71x** [`ciq_facts.json` → `net_debt_ebitda_x` = 1,237.8 ÷ 724.9] | 846.2 ÷ 677.6 = 1.25x; 846.2 ÷ 724.9 = 1.17x |
| **Debt / capital** | — | **40.7%** on borrowings-only · [lease-inclusive **44.2%**] | 2,519.8 ÷ (2,519.8 + 3,673.6 equity) = 2,519.8 ÷ 6,193.4 = 40.7%. Lease-inclusive: 2,911.4 ÷ 6,585.0 = 44.2% — which matches `CIQ Financials_Annual → Ratios` 'Total Debt/Capital' 0.442126 for the LTM column exactly [vendor export] |
| **Debt / equity** | — | **68.6%** on borrowings-only · [lease-inclusive **79.3%**] | 2,519.8 ÷ 3,673.6 = 68.6%. Lease-inclusive: 2,911.4 ÷ 3,673.6 = 79.3% — matches `CIQ Financials_Annual → Ratios` 'Total Debt/Equity' 0.792519 exactly [vendor export] |

**Cycle-position rows — mandatory here, because `business-model/07_business-quality.md` scores Cyclicality 22/100 (Weak) and classifies TTWO as "a cyclical, hit-driven owner of entertainment intellectual property".** The dominant cycle is the **release slate**, not the economy: FY26 revenue rose $1,022.8m (+18.2%) on five named franchise launches, then Q2 FY27 net bookings fell **18%** to $1,601.3m "simply because the company sat between launches" [`business-model/07_business-quality.md`, citing `FY26 10-K, MD&A` and `Q2 FY27 earnings release, 2026-11-09`].

| Net debt / EBITDA by cycle position (strict net debt 1,370.4) | Multiple | Cycle label |
|---|---:|---|
| On (D) company-guided FY2027 EBITDA, midpoint 1,053 | **1.30x** | **Coming peak** — the Grand Theft Auto VI launch year (title released **2026-11-19**). Forward company guidance, not realised |
| On (B) vendor LTM EBITDA to 2026-09-30, 724.9 | **1.89x** | **Latest — between launches.** Not the peak: 8.1% below FY2026's 788.5, and the whole LTM window sits **before** the GTA VI launch |
| On (A) company-defined LTM EBITDA, 677.6 | **2.02x** | **Latest — between launches**, on the filing-built company definition |
| On (C) mid-cycle / normalised EBITDA, 565.3 | **2.42x** | **Mid-cycle / normalised** — the company's own five-year average EBITDA. This is the central estimate; the 1.30x above is a floor, not a forecast |

So the honest range is **1.30x at the guided peak to 2.42x through the cycle**, with the latest realised reading at **1.89–2.02x**. A single-year figure headlined on its own would mislead in either direction here — and the direction is unusual: the latest period is *between* launches, so **the peak lies ahead, not behind**, which is the opposite of the usual cyclical trap.

## 6. Leverage Trend

Strict basis throughout (gross borrowings at carrying value − cash & equivalents). Every debt and cash figure below is read from the filings' own balance sheets and debt notes; the EBITDA row is the vendor series, labelled, because the company publishes no annual EBITDA.

| Metric | FY2024 (2024-03-31) | FY2025 (2025-03-31) | FY2026 (2026-03-31) | Latest (2026-09-30) | Direction |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| Gross debt (borrowings, carrying) | 3,082.9 | 3,661.1 | 2,518.0 | 2,519.8 | Fell 31.2% FY25→FY26, flat since |
| Cash & equivalents | 754.0 | 1,456.1 | 1,545.5 | 1,149.4 | Rose, then fell 25.6% in H1 FY27 |
| **Net debt (strict §15 basis)** | **2,328.9** | **2,205.0** | **972.5** | **1,370.4** | **Fell sharply, then rose again** |
| *Net debt (broad basis, memo)* | *2,306.9* | *2,195.6* | *528.7* | *846.2* | *Same shape* |
| EBITDA (vendor basis, annual / LTM) | 432.2 | 379.5 | 788.5 | 724.9 | — |
| **Net debt / EBITDA** (strict ÷ vendor EBITDA) | **5.39x** | **5.81x** | **1.23x** | **1.89x** | **Down then back up** |
| *Gross debt / EBITDA (same bases, memo)* | *7.13x* | *9.65x* | *3.19x* | *3.48x* | *Same shape* |
| Total stockholders' equity | 5,667.9 | 2,137.7 | 3,510.9 | 3,673.6 | Collapsed on impairments, then rebuilt by issuing shares |
| Operating-lease liabilities (off the debt line, US GAAP) | 451.1 | 444.8 | 440.3 | 391.6 | Slowly declining |

Sources: FY2024 and FY2025 balance sheets from `FY25 10-K, Consolidated Balance Sheets`; FY2026 from `FY26 10-K, Consolidated Balance Sheets` and `FY26 10-K, Note 11 (Debt)`; latest from `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Condensed Consolidated Balance Sheets` and `Note 10 (Debt)`; EBITDA row from `CIQ Financials_Annual → Income Statement`, EBITDA row, with the LTM value pinned at 724.9 by `ciq_facts.json` [vendor export]. The FY2024–FY2026 net-debt and leverage series matches `earnings/01_historical-financials.md` §1 line-for-line (2,328.9 / 2,205.0 / 972.5 and 5.39x / 5.81x / 1.23x), which reached them independently.

**Leverage more than halved in FY2026 and has since given back about a third of that, and the two moves had completely different causes.** The FY2026 fall from 5.81x to 1.23x came from **repaying $1,150.0m of notes — the $600.0m 2025 Notes on 2025-04-14 and the $550.0m 2026 Notes on 2026-03-28 — "with proceeds from our May 2025 equity issuance"** (5.5m shares at $225.00, net proceeds $1,192.8m), alongside EBITDA rising from 379.5 to 788.5 and operating cash flow swinging from $(45.2)m to $+624.3m [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt)`; `FY26 10-K, Note 12`; `FY26 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows`; `CIQ Financials_Annual → Income Statement` for EBITDA]. That is debt retired with **shareholders' money, not operating cash** — the point `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` makes, and the reason the share count rose 61.7% from 115.8m (2022-03-31) to 187.2m with **zero buybacks in FY2024, FY2025 and FY2026** [`FY26 10-K, Item 5`; `CIQ Historical Capitalization` — vendor export].

**The rise since — $397.9m of it — is all cash burn, no new borrowing, and it reconciles exactly.** Net debt went from 972.5 to 1,370.4, a rise of **397.9**, built as: cash & equivalents fell **396.1** (1,545.5 → 1,149.4) plus gross carrying debt rose **1.8** (2,518.0 → 2,519.8, purely discount amortization and the convertible's fair-value mark). The cash fall itself reconciles with no residual: operating cash flow **(173.5)** + investing **(259.9)** + financing **+30.4** + FX **(2.7)** = **(405.7)** total change in cash *and restricted cash*, less the **(9.6)** fall in restricted cash (92.6 → 83.0) = **(396.1)** ✔ [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Condensed Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows`; balance-sheet lines as above]. Inside the investing figure, **$80.4m simply moved from cash into bank time deposits** — which is why the broad basis rose less (528.7 → 846.2, a rise of 317.5), and 397.9 − 317.5 = **80.4** ✔, the same number. The real cash drains were the **negative $(173.5)m of operating cash flow** in the six months and **$163.3m of fixed-asset purchases** (which includes buying the London office building the company had been leasing, for a total capitalized cost of $91.8m on 2026-08-10) [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note on subsequent/other transactions, p.1036 of extract`]. **Nothing in the rise is new debt.**

---

## 6A. HoldCo / OpCo & Structural Subordination

**Applicable, but small and about to disappear.**

| Item | Evidence | Why It Matters |
|---|---|---|
| **Where debt sits (HoldCo vs OpCo)** | **$2,500.0 of Senior Notes (98.8% of borrowings) and the $1,000.0 revolver sit at the parent, Take-Two Interactive Software, Inc.** — "the Company's senior unsecured obligations [which] rank equally with all of our other existing and future unsubordinated obligations" [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt) — Senior Notes`]. The one exception: the **$29.4 of 2026 Convertible Notes are "senior unsecured indebtedness of Zynga, ranking pari passu with all of our other existing and future senior unsecured unsubordinated obligations of Zynga. As a result, the 2026 Convertible Notes are structurally senior to the indebtedness of the Company as to Zynga, its subsidiaries, and their respective assets."** Take-Two guarantees them, and that guarantee "rank[s] equally with all of" Take-Two's other senior unsecured obligations [`FY26 10-K, Note 11 (Debt) — Convertible Notes`] | **Structural subordination exists and is disclosed, and it is 1.2% of gross borrowings ($29.4 of $2,529.8). It matures 2026-12-15** — within weeks of this report — after which the structure is, on this pool's evidence, entirely parent-level unsecured. The subsidiaries named in the vendor profile (Zynga, Gearbox, Platygobian) issue no other debt disclosed in the filings [`CIQ Public Company Profile` — vendor export] |
| **Upstreaming constraints (dividend blockers, regulatory)** | **No dividend blocker, repatriation restriction or regulatory upstreaming block is disclosed in either filing.** The 2022 Credit Agreement's negative covenants run the other way — they "limit or restrict the Company and its subsidiaries' ability to… incur **subsidiary indebtedness**, grant liens, and dispose of all or substantially all assets" [`FY26 10-K, Note 11 (Debt) — Credit Agreement`]. On cash location the company states only that the $945.0 held abroad "is dispersed across various locations around the world… such dispersion meets the business and liquidity needs of our foreign affiliates. In addition, we expect to have the ability to generate sufficient cash domestically to support ongoing operations for the foreseeable future" [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Item 2 MD&A`] | The debt is at the entity that owns the group, so there is no HoldCo-cannot-reach-the-cash problem of the usual kind. The covenant restriction on **subsidiary** indebtedness is itself a protection against future structural subordination. **What is not disclosed:** any quantification of how much of the $945.0 offshore could be moved to the US, in what time, at what tax cost. Recorded as "Not disclosed in the data pool", not assumed to be free |
| **Material restricted / trapped cash** | **Restricted cash $83.0** (current 15.0 + long-term 68.0), restriction described as "dedicated accounts limited to the payment of certain internal royalty obligations" [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Condensed Consolidated Balance Sheets`; `FY26 10-K, Note 1`]. **Offshore cash $945.0 = 82.2% of cash & equivalents**, not described as restricted [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Item 2 MD&A`] | The $83.0 restricted balance is **excluded from every net-debt figure above** — it is not silently netted. Net debt is therefore **not** understated by restricted cash. The offshore concentration is a timing-and-tax question for `03_liquidity-runway`, not a net-debt adjustment: the company has not disclosed any legal barrier, and inventing a haircut would be inference presented as fact |

The undrawn committed revolver ($997.9 available of a $1,000.0 commitment) is **not** in the 1,673.6 — `03` adds it |

Build: FY2026 **760.6** (my build from FY26 10-K lines, because the company publishes no FY2026 full-year EBITDA) − H1 FY26 **342.2** (company-disclosed) + H1 FY27 **259.2** (company-disclosed) = 677.6. **Cycle position: latest, BETWEEN LAUNCHES — not peak and not trough.** The GTA VI launch (2026-11-19) falls *after* this window |

| **Reported (GAAP) profit anchor** | **LTM operating loss (180.8)**; FY2026 operating loss (104.2) | **There is no GAAP EBITDA.** Any "debt ÷ reported EBITDA" ratio is **not meaningful** — negative denominator. Every leverage multiple in this module is on a **non-GAAP** EBITDA; that caveat must propagate |

| **Credit rating — vendor-sourced only, not a rating action** | S&P Foreign Currency LT **BBB**; CIQ relative credit-health quartile **4 (bottom)** on all four of overall / operational / solvency / liquidity, against 25 Interactive Home Entertainment peers, LTM period ending 2026-09-30 | `CIQ Credit Health Panel → Summary`, financials updated 2026-11-10 — **tier-5 vendor export, not a rating-agency rationale or outlook.** No rating-agency report is in this pool; no rating trajectory may be inferred. Noted because the revolver's spread is **rating-determined** |

**Caveats every downstream agent must propagate:**
1. **Every leverage multiple here rests on a non-GAAP EBITDA.** GAAP operating profit is negative on both the latest full year and the LTM. There is no GAAP-EBITDA-based ratio to show.
2. **The canonical EBITDA (677.6) is partly engine-computed.** Its FY2026 component (760.6) is my own build on the company's published definition from the FY26 10-K's own lines, because the company does not publish a FY2026 full-year EBITDA in this pool. The H1 FY27 and H1 FY26 components (259.2 and 342.2) are company-disclosed.
3. **This is a cyclical, release-slate-driven business** (`business-model/07`, Cyclicality 22/100). **1.30x is a floor built on company guidance, 2.42x on the own five-year average is the central estimate.** Do not headline a single-year multiple without its cycle label.
4. **Source-check status: UNCHECKED, not verified clean** (no `numeric_reading_check` field on any of the 30 manifest sources). Every amount above was verified by a direct read of the frozen filing extract that reports it; nothing is quoted from a vendor tab under a filing's name.
5. **The vendor's net debt (1,237.8) and net leverage (1.71x) are on a different definition** — broad on cash, lease-inclusive on debt. They reconcile exactly to the canonical figures and are not in conflict, but they are **not interchangeable** with them.
6. **Not net cash.** TTWO is net debt on both bases (strict 1,370.4; broad 846.2), so the §24 Filter-3 net-cash credit does not apply here. The structure is also **not** evaluated against any "optimal leverage" frame — this module rejects it (MODULE_RULES Core Principle 8).

**No solvency verdict is made here.** Maturities (`02`), liquidity runway (`03`), coverage and covenant headroom (`04`), off-balance-sheet items (`05`), the downside stress test (`06`) and the solvency verdict (`99`) are other agents' work.

# Maturity Wall & Refinancing — TTWO

**Company:** Take-Two Interactive Software, Inc. (NasdaqGS:TTWO) · **Date:** 2026-11-19
**Reporting currency: USD, in millions** unless stated. **Reporting standard: US GAAP.** Fiscal year ends **31 March**, so "FY2028" means the year ending **2028-03-31**. Latest balance sheet and maturity table: **2026-09-30** (Q2 of FY2027).
**Filing regime:** US SEC — Form 10-K (annual), Form 10-Q (quarterly).

**Standing verification qualifier carried forward from `00_solvency-data-triage.md` §5 and `01_capital-structure-and-leverage.md`.** All 30 manifest sources in this frozen generation carry **no `numeric_reading_check` field at all** — they are **unchecked, not verified clean** — and no labelled extractor warning annotation appears in `GENERATION_ROOT/corpus.txt`, so there is no specific flagged amount to quarantine. The duty that transfers: **every material amount below was read directly from the frozen filing extract that reports it** — `10Q_2Q_0001628280-26-072254.txt` (Q2 FY27 10-Q), `10K_2025_26_0001628280-26-037434.txt` (FY26 10-K) — and the Capital IQ workbook tabs are used only as a vendor **read to reconcile against**, never as the authority for a filing's own number (CLAUDE.md §5). Every restating layer downstream must carry this unchecked-source qualifier forward.

**Upstream inputs used.** `01_capital-structure-and-leverage.md` (gross debt, instruments, canonical net debt and EBITDA bases — used verbatim); `earnings/01_historical-financials.md` (FCF available to repay, its capex definition); `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` (how the debt got here, refi-adjacent commentary). No cross-module input was missing.

## 1. Maturity Schedule

Reproduced **as filed**, from "the aggregate amount of maturities of our borrowings, as of September 30, 2026" [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt)`]. The filing's buckets are **fiscal years ending 31 March**, so each row's calendar window is spelled out. Instrument attribution is mine, matched from the dated maturity of each note in the same note; the per-instrument dates also appear in `CIQ Financials_Quarterly → Capital-Structure-Details` [vendor export, `Source: Q2 2027 filed Nov-09-2026`] and agree exactly.

| Period | Amount Due | % of Total Debt | Instrument(s) | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---|---|
| **Within 12 months** — FY2027 remaining (Oct-2026 → Mar-2027) | **29.4** | **1.2%** | 2026 Convertible Notes, 0.00%, due **2026-12-15** — issued by **Zynga Inc. (OpCo)**, Take-Two guaranteed, unsecured | `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt)` — maturities table and Convertible Notes paragraph |
| **Year 2** — FY2028 (Apr-2027 → Mar-2028) | **1,400.0** | **55.3%** | 2027 Notes **600.0**, 3.70%, due **2027-04-14** *and* 2028 Notes **800.0**, 4.95%, due **2028-03-28** — both Take-Two senior unsecured | `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt)` — maturities table and the Long-term / Short-term debt component tables |
| **Year 3** — FY2029 (Apr-2028 → Mar-2029) | **—** | **0.0%** | None | `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt)` |
| **Year 4** — FY2030 (Apr-2029 → Mar-2030) | **300.0** | **11.9%** | 2029 Notes, 5.40%, due **2029-06-12** — Take-Two senior unsecured | `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt)` |
| **Year 5** — FY2031 (Apr-2030 → Mar-2031) | **—** | **0.0%** | None drawn. The **$1,000.0 committed revolver matures 2030-05-19** (inside FY2031) but is **undrawn**, so it contributes nothing to this schedule | `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt) — Credit Agreement` |
| **Thereafter** (FY2032+) | **800.0** | **31.6%** | 2032 Notes **500.0**, 4.00%, due **2032-04-14** *and* 2034 Notes **300.0**, 5.60%, due **2034-06-12** | `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt)` |
| **Total (principal)** | **2,529.4** | **100%** | | `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt)` |
| *memo:* Fair value adjustments | *0.4* | — | The convertible is carried at fair value under the ASC 825 option | `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt)` |
| *memo:* **Total face value** | ***2,529.8*** | — | | `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt)` |

**The schedule ties to `01`'s gross debt, with the reconciling item named.** `01` designates gross debt as **$2,519.8** (borrowings at balance-sheet **carrying** value: short-term debt, net 629.2 + long-term debt, net 1,890.6) and the face-value variant as **$2,529.8**. The bridge, with no residual:

> schedule principal **2,529.4** + fair-value adjustment **0.4** = face **2,529.8** − unamortized discount and issuance cost **10.0** (9.4 long-term + 0.6 short-term) = carrying **2,519.8** ✔

Two things sit **outside** this table and must not be read as maturities:
- **Operating-lease liabilities $391.6** at 2026-09-30 (current 66.9 + non-current 324.7). Under **US GAAP** these are not in the borrowings line or this table; under IFRS 16 they would be. They **amortise through rent** rather than coming due as a refinancing event — the FY26 lease note gives undiscounted payments of 548.5 spread FY27 92.9 / FY28 86.0 / FY29 84.0 / FY30 74.6 / FY31 64.4 / thereafter 146.6, less imputed interest (108.2) [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Condensed Consolidated Balance Sheets`; `FY26 10-K, Note 13 (Leases)`]. The vendor labels them "Capital Lease… Secured: Yes" with no maturity date, which is a vendor convention, not a filed secured borrowing [`CIQ Financials_Quarterly → Capital-Structure-Details` — vendor export].
- **The undrawn revolver.** $1,000.0 committed, unsecured, **not** borrowing-base; **$997.9 available** at 2026-09-30 ($997.7 at 2026-03-31) with $2.1 of letters of credit outstanding and no borrowings [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt) — Credit Agreement`].

**One prior-period cross-check.** The same table as of 2026-03-31 reads FY2027 29.4 / FY2028 1,400.0 / FY2029 — / FY2030 300.0 / FY2031 — / Thereafter 800.0 / Total 2,529.4, with fair-value adjustments 0.6 and total face value 2,530.0 [`FY26 10-K, Note 11 (Debt)`]. Nothing in the wall has moved in six months except the $0.2 fair-value mark on the convertible.

**Vendor reconciliation, and which source is the authority.** `ciq_facts.json → debt_maturity_wall` reports "dated debt 2,530 of 2,970 total principal by maturity — 2026 30; 2027 600; 2028 800; 2029 300; 2032 500; 2034 300; nearest 2026-12-15, WAM ~3.4y from 2026-03-31; 0 floating / 2,970 fixed; amortizing leases (no refi maturity) 440" [`CIQ Financials → Capital Structure Details` — tier-5 vendor export]. Reconciled: the vendor is on **calendar** maturity years (not fiscal), is computed from the **2026-03-31** as-reported block, rounds the convertible to 30 (filing: 29.4), and includes the 440.3 of operating leases in its 2,970 total. Its instrument-by-instrument principals match the filing exactly. Per §4/§5 the **10-Q table as of 2026-09-30 is the authority**; the vendor figure is cited for its own number and is not overridden. The vendor's ~3.4-year WAM is on its own basis and date; my filing-based figure is below.

## 2. Maturity Profile Metrics

Computed on the filed **principal** of 2,529.4 (so the top and bottom of every share are the same measurement — principal over principal, §15 matched basis). All windows are rolling from a stated measurement date, because the filed table's fiscal-year buckets are not 12/24/36-month windows.

| Metric | Value |
|---|---:|
| **Weighted-average maturity (years)** — principal-weighted, from the balance-sheet date **2026-09-30** | **2.93** |
| *same, from today **2026-11-19*** | *2.79* |
| **% due within 12 months** (to 2027-09-30): 29.4 + 600.0 = **629.4** | **24.9%** |
| **% due within 24 months** (to 2028-09-30): + 800.0 = **1,429.4** | **56.5%** |
| **% due within 36 months** (to 2029-09-30): + 300.0 = **1,729.4** | **68.4%** |
| **Largest single maturity year (and amount)** | **FY2028 (year ending 2028-03-31): $1,400.0 = 55.3% of principal** |

Measured from **today (2026-11-19)** the three windows are unchanged in amount and share — the convertible (2026-12-15) and the 2027 Notes (2027-04-14) are both still inside 12 months, the 2028 Notes (2028-03-28) inside 24, and the 2029 Notes (2029-06-12) inside 36.

**The $1,400.0 "wall" is one fiscal year but two payments eleven and a half months apart — say both.** The fiscal-year table concentrates the 2027 Notes and the 2028 Notes into FY2028 only because the 2027 Notes fall due **14 days after that fiscal year begins**. The actual cash dates are **2027-04-14 ($600.0)** and **2028-03-28 ($800.0)** [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt)`]. The concentration is real on a fiscal-year view and materially softer on a payment-date view; both readings must travel together, because the single-year figure overstates how much has to be funded at one moment and the payment-date view understates how little room there is between them.

**Build of the WAM, so it can be rebuilt (§15 aggregates travel with their build).** From 2026-09-30, years to maturity × principal: 29.4 × 0.21 + 600.0 × 0.54 + 800.0 × 1.49 + 300.0 × 2.70 + 500.0 × 5.54 + 300.0 × 7.70 = 7,416.9 ÷ 2,529.4 = **2.93 years**. Maturity dates and principals from `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt)`; the arithmetic is mine.

## 3. Rate Exposure

| Metric | Value | Source |
|---|---:|---|
| **Fixed-rate share** of drawn borrowings | **100.0%** (2,529.4 of 2,529.4 principal) | Every instrument carries a stated fixed annual interest rate in `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt)` (3.70 / 4.95 / 5.40 / 4.00 / 5.60 / 0.00%); the vendor agrees, "0 floating / 2,970 fixed" [`ciq_facts.json → debt_maturity_wall` — vendor export] |

| **Weighted-average coupon** — Senior Notes only ($2,500.0) | **4.59%** | (600×3.70 + 800×4.95 + 300×5.40 + 500×4.00 + 300×5.60) ÷ 2,500 = 11,480 ÷ 2,500. Coupons from `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt)`; arithmetic mine. Matches `01` §7 exactly |
| *same, across all $2,529.4 including the 0.00% convertible* | *4.54%* | 11,480 ÷ 2,529.4 |
| **Current market refi rate — (a) TTWO's own bonds, pool-sourced** | **5.48% weighted-average** (range **4.96%** at 0.5y to **6.17%** at 7.7y) | **Engine-computed yield-to-maturity from the filing's own Level-2 fair values at 2026-09-30** [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt)`]. Build and assumptions below |
| **Current market refi rate — (b) web-sourced benchmark, current** | **~5.95% (2-year) to ~6.04% (5-year)** | 2-year US Treasury **4.79%** and 5-year **4.88%** [`Web: US Treasury yields, 2026-11-19 (indicative, unverified)`] **plus** ICE BofA **BBB** US Corporate Index option-adjusted spread **116 bps** [`Web: FRED BAMLC0A4CBBB, 2026-11-18 (indicative, unverified)`] |
| **Estimated refi cost step-up (bps)** | **+89 bps** on basis (a) · **+136 to +145 bps** on basis (b) → **roughly +90 to +145 bps** | (a) 5.478% − 4.592% = 0.886% = 89 bps. (b) 5.95% − 4.59% = 136 bps (2y); 6.04% − 4.59% = 145 bps (5y) |

**Build of basis (a), so a reader can reproduce it.** The 10-Q discloses a Level-2 fair value against each note's face amount. Dividing gives a clean price; I add accrued interest to the semi-annual coupon dates implied by each maturity date (30/360), then solve the semi-annual yield to maturity at a 2026-09-30 settlement:

| Note | Face | Fair value (Level 2) | Clean price | + accrued | Coupon | Years to maturity | **Implied YTM** | vs coupon |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 2027 Notes | 600.0 | 596.1 | 99.35 | 101.06 | 3.70% | 0.54 | **4.96%** | +126 bp |
| 2028 Notes | 800.0 | 796.5 | 99.56 | 99.59 | 4.95% | 1.49 | **5.27%** | +32 bp |
| 2029 Notes | 300.0 | 298.3 | 99.43 | 101.05 | 5.40% | 2.70 | **5.63%** | +23 bp |
| 2032 Notes | 500.0 | 454.8 | 90.96 | 92.80 | 4.00% | 5.54 | **5.94%** | +194 bp |
| 2034 Notes | 300.0 | 289.6 | 96.53 | 98.21 | 5.60% | 7.70 | **6.17%** | +57 bp |
| **Weighted average** | **2,500.0** | **1,839.2 + 596.1 = 2,435.3** | — | — | **4.59%** | **2.96** | **5.48%** | **+89 bp** |

Face amounts and fair values are read from `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt)`; **the yields are my own calculation, not a filed figure** — labelled engine-computed. Two honest limits on it: the filing does not state coupon payment dates, so the accrual is inferred from each maturity date; and the fair values are **as of 2026-09-30**, about seven weeks stale at this report's date. That staleness is why basis (b) is carried alongside rather than discarded.

**The basis mismatch in (b), named rather than buried (§15 matched basis).** The 116 bp figure is an **index-level option-adjusted spread across all BBB US corporate bonds of every duration**, not a matching-tenor spread on TTWO's own paper. Adding it to a single-point Treasury yield is an approximation, and the two web inputs are from different dates (2026-11-19 and 2026-11-18). Both legs are therefore labelled indicative and unverified and the resulting +136/+145 bp step-up is a bound, not a measurement. The internal consistency check is reassuring but not proof: the own-bond read (a) at ~1.5 years was 5.27% on 2026-09-30, against the ~5.95% two-year indicative figure now, so the direction of travel is a wider cost, consistent with the 2-year Treasury having firmed.

**What the floating share means if rates move — the answer is "nothing on the drawn debt, and the exposure runs the other way."** With 0% of drawn borrowings floating and no swaps, a **+200 bp** rate shock changes drawn interest cost by **$0.0**. Two real exposures remain, and neither is a cost: (i) **if** the revolver were fully drawn at its $997.9 availability, +200 bp would add **$20.0 a year** — a contingent figure, not a current one; (ii) the company's rate exposure is predominantly on the **asset** side — "$524.2 of short-term investments… also had $1,149.4 of cash and cash equivalents that are comprised primarily of money market funds and bank-time deposits," on which it concluded "there was no material interest rate risk exposure… or liquidity as of September 30, 2026" [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Item 3`]. Interest **income** was $85.1 in FY2026 [`01` §5a, from `FY26 10-K, Note 17 (Interest and Other, Net)`], so **falling** rates, not rising ones, are the adverse direction for TTWO's net interest line. That is the opposite of the usual floating-rate worry and must not be restated as "no rate risk".

**One rating-linked term to carry forward.** The revolver's spread is "determined by the Company's credit rating" [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10`], so a downgrade raises the cost of the one facility the company would lean on in a closed market. The only rating in the pool is a vendor indication — S&P Foreign Currency LT **BBB** [`CIQ Credit Health Panel → Summary`, LTM period ending 2026-09-30, financials updated 2026-11-10 — tier-5 vendor export]. **No rating-agency rationale or outlook is in the pool**, so no rating trajectory may be inferred, and the size of any pricing step is not disclosed. No commitment fee is disclosed anywhere in the pool either.

---

## 4. Refinancing Exposure

### Refi Funding Plan (no speculation)

Next-24-month maturities measured from 2026-09-30 (unchanged measured from today): **$1,429.4** = 29.4 (2026-12-15) + 600.0 (2027-04-14) + 800.0 (2028-03-28).

|---|---:|---|
| **Cash on hand** | **1,149.4** cash & equivalents; **1,673.6** on the **gross-liquidity** basis including 524.2 of liquid short-term investments (basis labelled per §15) | `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Condensed Consolidated Balance Sheets`; `Note 5 (Short-Term Investments)` — the 524.2 is "primarily… bank time deposits with maturities greater than 90 days", so it is liquid but **not** a cash equivalent. **Restricted cash of 83.0 is excluded** and stays excluded. Concentration flag, not a restriction: **945.0 (82.2% of cash & equivalents) is held offshore** [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Item 2 MD&A`] |
| **Forecast FCF** (company guidance — forward, not realised) | **> ~710** for FY2027 (CFO **over 1,000.0** − capex **approximately 290.0**) | `Q2 FY27 earnings release, 2026-11-09, FY2027 Outlook table` ("Net cash provided by operating activities **over $1,000**; Capital expenditures **approximately $290**"); the capex figure is independently in the **filing**: "In fiscal year 2027, we anticipate capital expenditures to be approximately $290.0" [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Item 2 MD&A — Capital Expenditures`]. On `earnings/01`'s broader capex definition (fixed assets **plus** asset acquisitions, 27.4 in FY2026) the guided FCF is **> ~683**. **Recent run-rate contradicts the guidance and is shown beside it:** LTM FCF to 2026-09-30 **62.6** (CFO 367.1 − total capex 304.5) [`earnings/01_historical-financials.md` §1, §8], and **H1 FY27 FCF was −336.8** (CFO −173.5 − fixed-asset capex 163.3) [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Condensed Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows`] |
| **Revolver availability** (availability is known, so it may be counted) | **997.9 available** of a **1,000.0 committed** unsecured five-year facility, **not** borrowing-base, maturing **2030-05-19** with two one-year extension options; 2.1 of letters of credit outstanding | `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt) — Credit Agreement` (availability table). **Conditional, and the condition is named:** drawing is subject to the 2022 Credit Agreement's maximum **Leverage Ratio** covenant (3.75:1.00 for quarters ending 2025-12-31 and thereafter) [`FY25 10-K, Exhibit 10.41, §6.05`]. Whether that covenant currently has headroom is `04_coverage-and-covenants`' calculation, not asserted here |
| **Asset-sale proceeds** | **Unknown — none announced or authorized in the data pool.** Do not assume any | No divestiture proceeds in FY2026 (FY2025: 32.7) [`FY26 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows`]. The 2026-11 subsequent-events note discloses only "a reorganization involving certain intangible assets to simplify our global operating structure", with no proceeds [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 16 (Subsequent Events)`] |
| **New debt issuance** | **Unknown — no committed or announced issuance in the data pool.** Do not assume any | No proceeds from issuance of debt in FY2026 or in the six months to 2026-09-30 [`FY26 10-K` and `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Statements of Cash Flows`]. Nothing in `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 16 (Subsequent Events)`. **There is also no TTWO material-event (8-K) feed in the pool**, so any financing announced after 2026-11-09 would not be visible here [`00_solvency-data-triage.md` §5, limitation 7] |
| *memo — uncommitted, deliberately excluded* | *215.0 receivables-purchase facility (**uncommitted**), unused in FY2026 and in the six months to 2026-09-30; plus uncommitted incremental revolver capacity of the greater of 250.0 and 35.0% of Consolidated Adjusted EBITDA* | `FY26 10-K, Note 2 (Accounts Receivable sale program)`; `FY26 10-K, Note 11 (Debt)`. **Excluded from every figure above** per MODULE_RULES Core Principle 4 — an uncommitted line can be pulled exactly when it is needed |

### Cash-finance-cost bridge (MODULE_RULES Calculation Standard 8) — applied before calling anything self-funded

**Chosen FCF basis:** reported **§15 FCF = CFO − total capex**. Reported and any adjusted measure are shown separately, and the classification of cash finance costs is established from the source statements rather than assumed.

| Step | Finding | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Reported FCF (§15), FY2026 | **434.1** = CFO 624.3 − total capex 190.2 (fixed assets 162.8 + asset acquisitions 27.4). On fixed-asset capex only: 461.5 | `FY26 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows`; definition and build from `earnings/01_historical-financials.md` §1 |
| Reported FCF (§15), LTM to 2026-09-30 | **62.6** = CFO 367.1 − total capex 304.5. On fixed-asset capex only: 98.0. (CFO bridge 624.3 − 83.7 + (−173.5) = 367.1, which matches `ciq_facts.json → ltm_ocf_m` = 367.1 exactly) | `earnings/01` §1, §8; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Condensed Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows`; `ciq_facts.json → ltm_ocf_m` [vendor export, reconciled] |
| **Cash finance costs identified** | **Interest paid 153.0** (FY2026), 147.1 (FY2025), 137.0 (FY2024); **Senior Notes interest payments 57.4** in the six months to 2026-09-30; **operating-lease cash payments 82.4** (FY2026) | `FY26 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows — Supplemental data`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt)`; `FY26 10-K, Note 13 (Leases)` |
| **Classification — established, not assumed** | **All of them are inside operating cash flow.** The FY26 10-K's investing section contains no interest or lease line, and its financing section contains only share-settlement tax, stock issuance, convertible settlement, debt proceeds, cost of debt, debt repayment and earn-out — **no interest payment**. The indirect-method statement adds back **accrued** interest expense (151.4) and the cash payment runs out through the change in "Accounts payable, accrued expenses and other liabilities" (−365.3); the supplemental "Interest paid 153.0" disclosure sits with the operating statement and is the cash amount. Operating leases are operating cash flows under US GAAP (noncash lease expense 56.2 added back; 82.4 actually paid) | Direct read of `FY26 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows` (all three sections) and `FY26 10-K, Note 13 (Leases)` |
| **Cash finance costs ABSENT from the chosen FCF** | **$0.0 — nothing is added.** Cash interest and lease payments are already deducted inside CFO, so re-adding them would charge the same payment twice, overstate the funding need and understate the cover | Follows from the row above |
| **Residual gaps retained, not resolved** | (i) The 10-Q carries **no supplemental "interest paid" line**, so H1 FY27 cash finance costs are evidenced only as the **57.4** of Senior Notes interest payments; (ii) **revolver commitment and letter-of-credit fees are not disclosed anywhere in the pool** — not quantified, and not assumed to be zero, though the facility is undrawn with only 2.1 of LCs outstanding; (iii) **availability gap:** 945.0 (82.2%) of cash & equivalents is offshore and the timing and tax cost of moving it to the US is **"Not disclosed in the data pool"** | `Q2 FY27 10-Q` (absence of a supplemental cash-flow disclosure); `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Item 2 MD&A`; `01_capital-structure-and-leverage.md` §6A |

These three residuals, and the unchecked-source status at the top of this report, travel into `03_liquidity-runway` and `06_downside-stress-test` with the reconciled basis: **cash interest and lease payments are inside CFO and must not be re-added**, and the small unquantified facility fees and the offshore-cash timing question remain open.

### The cover, arithmetic shown

| Window | Due | ÷ cash & equivalents 1,149.4 | ÷ gross liquidity 1,673.6 | ÷ gross liquidity + revolver 2,671.5 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| **12 months** | **629.4** | **1.83x** | **2.66x** | 4.24x |
| **24 months** | **1,429.4** | 0.80x | **1.17x** | **1.87x** |
| **36 months** | **1,729.4** | 0.67x | 0.97x | 1.54x |

**Is the near-term wall covered by cash and FCF, or does it need market access?** The next 12 months' $629.4 is covered **1.83x by cash & equivalents alone** ($1,149.4), before any FCF, before the $524.2 of short-term investments and before the revolver [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Condensed Consolidated Balance Sheets`; `Note 10 (Debt)`]. The next 24 months' $1,429.4 is covered **1.17x by cash plus liquid short-term investments** ($1,673.6) with no market access at all, and **1.87x** if the $997.9 of committed, non-borrowing-base revolver availability is included — so on the balance sheet as filed, the wall does **not** require an open bond market. What it does require is that the cash be replenished: paying the whole 24-month wall out of liquidity would leave about **$244.2** on the balance sheet, against realised **LTM FCF of only $62.6** and a **−$336.8** FCF half-year just reported, so the practical funding of the 2028 Notes rests on the company's guided **FY2027 CFO of over $1,000.0 and FCF of more than ~$710** landing — which in turn rests on one product cycle, Grand Theft Auto VI, released **2026-11-19** and with **no reported period in this pool**. On rating posture and recent refi activity, the only rating available is a vendor indication of **S&P BBB** with no agency rationale or outlook in the pool, and the vendor's relative credit-health scores put TTWO in the **bottom quartile on all four of overall, operational, solvency and liquidity against 25 interactive-entertainment peers** [`CIQ Credit Health Panel → Summary`, LTM ending 2026-09-30 — tier-5 vendor export]; recent activity has been **repayment, not refinancing** — $600.0 of 2025 Notes repaid 2025-04-14 and $550.0 of 2026 Notes repaid 2026-03-28, both "with proceeds from our May 2025 equity issuance", and the revolver was amended on 2025-05-19 to lift the commitment from $750.0 to $1,000.0 and extend it to 2030-05-19, which is a completed, committed refinancing of the facility itself [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt)`; `FY26 10-K, Note 11 (Debt)`]. Because **0% of drawn borrowings float and no swaps are disclosed**, none of the interest cost on existing debt reprices if rates move; the repricing happens only when a note is refinanced, at roughly **+90 to +145 bps** (§3), and the asset side — $1,673.6 of cash and deposits earning $85.1 of interest income in FY2026 — reprices in the *opposite* direction.

**Conclusion: "self-funded / low refi risk"** — on the specific evidence that the 24-month wall of $1,429.4 is covered 1.17x by cash plus liquid short-term investments with no market access and 1.87x including $997.9 of known, committed, non-borrowing-base revolver availability, and that 100% of drawn borrowings are fixed-rate, unsecured and free of any borrowing base. **The qualifier that must travel with that label:** 56.5% of principal falls due within 24 months and the weighted-average maturity is only **2.93 years**, so self-funding is a statement about the cash balance at 2026-09-30, not about cash generation — realised LTM FCF is **$62.6**, and the company's guided FY2027 FCF of **more than ~$710** is a forecast resting on a single launch. Drawing the revolver is also conditional on the 3.75x maximum-leverage covenant, which `04` computes.

---

## 5. Refinancing Read

The wall is near and concentrated but pre-funded: **$629.4 (24.9%) comes due within 12 months and $1,429.4 (56.5%) within 24**, on a weighted-average maturity of just **2.93 years**, with the single largest bucket being **$1,400.0 (55.3%) inside fiscal 2028** — though that is two payments, **$600.0 on 2027-04-14 and $800.0 on 2028-03-28**, eleven and a half months apart, not one cliff; against that sits **$1,149.4 of cash, $524.2 of liquid short-term investments and $997.9 of committed undrawn revolver availability**, all at 2026-09-30 [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt)`, `Condensed Consolidated Balance Sheets`]. Refinancing rather than repaying would cost roughly **+90 to +145 bps** over the **4.59%** weighted-average coupon — about **$12.5–20.3 a year** on the $1,400.0 fiscal-2028 wall, or **$22.5–36.7** if the whole $2,529.4 were refinanced at today's indicative level, which is 8–14% of FY2026's $151.4 interest expense and small beside guided FY2027 EBITDA of $1,024–1,082 (a company forecast, non-GAAP, on the company's own definition). The single biggest refinancing risk is not the market: it is that **the cash paying this wall is replenished almost entirely by one product cycle** — realised LTM free cash flow (CFO minus total capex) is **$62.6** and the latest half-year was **−$336.8**, so if the Grand Theft Auto VI cycle launched 2026-11-19 does not convert into the guided **over $1,000.0 of operating cash flow**, TTWO either drains liquidity to roughly **$244.2** by 2028 or goes to market from the **bottom credit-health quartile of its 25-peer group** on a vendor panel, with a revolver whose spread is set by a credit rating that has **no agency rationale or outlook in this pool**.

**Does it survive the next 12 months under market closure (no new unsecured issuance)? Yes, on the best available evidence — with the assumptions labelled.** Only **$629.4** falls due in that window, against **$1,149.4** of cash & equivalents (1.83x) and **$1,673.6** including short-term investments (2.66x), with the **$997.9** revolver untouched and committed to 2030-05-19 [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 10 (Debt)`]. Even on a deliberately hostile **zero-mitigation bound** — assuming the H1 FY27 cash burn simply repeats for a full year (−$336.8 per half = −$673.6, which is an assumption, contradicted by the company's own guidance and by the launch falling inside the period) and assuming the full $629.4 is paid in cash — liquidity still ends at about **$1,673.6 − 673.6 − 629.4 = $370.6**, plus the undrawn revolver. Two conditions carried forward rather than claimed resolved: the revolver is drawable only if the 2022 Credit Agreement's maximum **Leverage Ratio** covenant of 3.75:1.00 holds (`04`'s calculation, not asserted here), and **82.2% of cash & equivalents ($945.0) sits offshore** with no disclosed legal barrier but also no disclosed timing or tax cost of repatriation [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Item 2 MD&A`]. One management claim that does **not** reconcile and is therefore not relied on: on the Q2 FY27 call the Executive Chairman said the balance sheet "is aimed at being very conservative and very strong. By year-end, we expect to be in a net cash position… right now, we're geared at less than 1x LTM EBITDA" [`Take-Two Q2 FY2027 earnings call, 2026-11-09`]. The "less than 1x" is below **every** basis computed in `01` — strict net debt/EBITDA **2.02x** (company-defined LTM EBITDA) and **1.89x** (vendor LTM EBITDA), broad-basis **1.25x / 1.17x**, and the vendor's broad-and-lease-inclusive **1.71x** — and the basis behind it is not stated, so it is recorded as an unreconciled management figure, not as evidence.

**Out-of-scope boundary.** No solvency verdict, liquidity runway, covenant headroom, stress result, probability, valuation or rating is made here — those belong to `03`, `04`, `06`, `99`, the valuation module and the master synthesizer.

**Sources used for the two web-sourced inputs (indicative, unverified, dated):** [US Treasury yields, 2026-11-19 — stockinsky.com/rates/us](https://stockinsky.com/rates/us) · [Treasury rates, 2026-11-18 — Forbes Advisor](https://www.forbes.com/advisor/investing/treasury-rates/) · [ICE BofA BBB US Corporate Index OAS, 2026-11-18 — FRED BAMLC0A4CBBB](https://fred.stlouisfed.org/series/BAMLC0A4CBBB)

# Liquidity Runway — TTWO

**Company:** Take-Two Interactive Software, Inc. (NasdaqGS:TTWO) · **Date:** 2026-11-19
**Reporting currency: USD, in millions** unless stated. **Reporting standard: US GAAP.** Fiscal year ends **31 March**, so "FY2027" is the year ending 2027-03-31. Balance-sheet date for every liquidity figure: **2026-09-30** (Q2 of FY2027). **Filing regime:** US SEC — Form 10-K (annual), Form 10-Q (quarterly).

**The 12-month window used throughout is 2026-10-01 → 2027-09-30** (twelve months from the latest balance sheet). Measured from today (2026-11-19) the same two debt maturities are still inside twelve months — the 2026 Convertible Notes on 2026-12-15 and the 2027 Notes on 2027-04-14 — so the maturity amount is unchanged on either measurement date [`02_maturity-wall-and-refinancing.md` §2].

**Standing verification qualifier carried forward from `00_solvency-data-triage.md` §5, `01_capital-structure-and-leverage.md` and `02_maturity-wall-and-refinancing.md`.** All 30 manifest sources in this frozen generation carry **no `numeric_reading_check` field at all** — they are **unchecked, not verified clean** — and no labelled extractor warning annotation appears in `GENERATION_ROOT/corpus.txt`, so there is no specific flagged amount to quarantine. The duty that transfers: **every material amount below was read directly from the frozen filing extract that reports it** — `10Q_2Q_0001628280-26-072254.txt` (Q2 FY27 10-Q), `10Q_1Q_0001628280-26-054870.txt` (Q1 FY27 10-Q), `10K_2025_26_0001628280-26-037434.txt` (FY26 10-K), `10K_2024_25_0001628280-25-026694.txt` (FY25 10-K, which carries the credit-agreement exhibit) — and the Capital IQ workbook tabs are used only as a vendor **read to reconcile against**, never as the authority for a filing's own number (CLAUDE.md §5). Every restating layer downstream must carry this unchecked-source qualifier forward.

**Upstream inputs used.** `01_capital-structure-and-leverage.md` (cash, short-term investments, restricted cash, gross/net debt, EBITDA bases — taken verbatim with their labels); `02_maturity-wall-and-refinancing.md` (the **next-12-month maturity figure of 629.4** and its cash-finance-cost bridge — reused, not rebuilt); `04_coverage-and-covenants.md` (there is **no minimum-liquidity covenant**, and a full revolver draw would not breach the one maintenance covenant); `05_off-balance-sheet-and-contingencies.md` (commitments and contingent claims on cash). Cross-module: `earnings/01_historical-financials.md` (CFO, capex, FCF definition) and `earnings/06_earnings-quality.md` (cash-backing of earnings — Earnings-quality **48/100**, LTM CFO/EBITDA **50.6%**). No declared input was missing.

**No partial-data rule and no score cap binds.** Committed-facility detail and stated availability are disclosed; audited and interim cash-flow statements are both present [`00_solvency-data-triage.md` §5–§6]. The qualifiers below are real limits, but none of them is one of the MODULE_RULES missing-data triggers.

The incremental revolver basket is the greater of 250.0 and 35.0% of Consolidated Adjusted EBITDA = 279.0 on `04`'s 797.2 covenant-EBITDA build, and is also uncommitted. **Excluded per MODULE_RULES Core Principle 4** — an uncommitted line can be pulled exactly when it is needed | `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Item 2 MD&A — Accounts Receivable sale program`; `FY26 10-K, Note 2`; `FY26 10-K, Note 11 (Debt)`; `04` §2 |

**Nothing is subtracted for a minimum-liquidity requirement, because none exists.** `04` searched the full credit-agreement text and found **no minimum-liquidity, no minimum-net-worth, no interest-coverage and no fixed-charge covenant**; the Senior Notes indenture carries no maintenance financial covenant at all [`04_coverage-and-covenants.md` §2, citing `FY25 10-K, Exhibit 10.41` and `FY26 10-K, Note 11 (Debt)`]. So MODULE_RULES' "subtract the minimum-liquidity requirement" step has nothing to apply to.

**Where liquidity may still be overstated or understated, stated plainly.**
- **Overstated, by an amount not disclosed:** 945.0 of the cash sits offshore. There is no disclosed legal barrier, but there is also no disclosed timetable or tax cost for bringing it home, and the company says that cost is "not practicable to estimate". Recorded as a **timing-and-tax question**, not netted out — inventing a haircut would be inference presented as fact.
- **Understated, slightly:** the 494.0 of uncommitted capacity is excluded by rule, and the company does list the receivables facility among the levers it may use "depending on cash flow needs and market conditions".
